On the distribution of mixed Hodge loci
Nazim Khelifa

Abstract
Let 𝕍 \mathbb{V} be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures over a smooth and irreducible complex algebraic variety S S . We show that if the typical Hodge locus HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) typ \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}} of 𝕍 \mathbb{V} is non-empty, the full Hodge locus HL ⁡ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes}) is dense in S S for the Zariski topology. In an other direction, we show that if the associated graded variation Gr ⁡ ( 𝕍 ) \mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V}) for the weight filtration has large monodromy and level at least 3 3 in the sense of Baldi-Klingler-Ullmo, the typical Hodge locus of 𝕍 \mathbb{V} is empty, and the full Hodge locus of 𝕍 \mathbb{V} is a strict Zariski-closed subset of S S , at least if one restricts to its factorwise positive dimensional part, improving a classical result of Brosnan-Pearlstein-Schnell in this situation. These results follow from a detailed study of the transverse part HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) trans \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}} of the Hodge locus of S S for 𝕍 \mathbb{V} , a subset which contains HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) typ \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}} and whose Zariski-density in S S is equivalent, under the Zilber-Pink conjecture for 𝕍 \mathbb{V} , to the Zariski-density of HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) typ \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}} . We show that non-emptiness of HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) trans \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}} is equivalent to its Zariski-density in S S , we completely classify variations whose transverse Hodge locus HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) trans \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}} is Zariski-dense, and we prove an independent criterion ensuring that HL ​ ( S , 𝕍 ⊗ ) trans \mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}} is empty.



Introduction

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures over a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Our main object of study will be the Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. It is the set of points
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
such that some rational tensor
\zeta_{s}\in T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{s,\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus_{a,b\geqslant 0}(\mathbb{V}_{s,\mathbb{Q}})^{\otimes a}\otimes(\mathbb{V}_{s,\mathbb{Q}}^{\vee})^{\otimes b}
satisfies:

•

\zeta_{s}
is a Hodge class for the natural
\mathbb{Q}
-mixed Hodge structure on
T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{s,\mathbb{Q}})
;

•

there exists a continuous path
\tau:[0,1]\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
\tau(0)=s
and the parallel transport of
\zeta_{s}
to
T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{\gamma(1),\mathbb{Q}})
along
\tau
is not a Hodge class for the natural mixed Hodge structure on
T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{\gamma(1),\mathbb{Q}})
.

A strong motivation for the study of the Hodge locus is Beilinson’s Hodge conjecture for higher cycles (
[
11
]
) which, for a variation arising as the cohomology of a family of smooth quasi-projective varieties, predicts that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
is the locus where some self-product of the corresponding fiber carries a higher cycle that does not spread over the generic fiber (see for instance
[
3
,
2
]
and references therein).

A priori
the Hodge locus is merely a countable union of strict complex-analytic subvarieties of
S
. A fundamental fact due to Brosnan-Pearlstein-Schnell in this generality (building on and generalizing a classical result of Cattani-Deligne-Kaplan
[
23
]
and a series of work
[
17
,
19
,
20
]
of the first two authors) is:

Theorem 1.1

(
[
18
]
)
.

The Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
is a countable union of strict algebraic subvarieties of
S
.

The present paper belongs to a line of works aiming to go one step further in the study of the distribution of Hodge locus in
S
, building on the intersection theoretic description of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
proposed in
[
39
]
.

The Zilber-Pink program and first results

In
[
39
]
, Klingler described
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
as a countable union of intersection loci, using the period map associated to
\mathbb{V}
(see. Sect.
4
for an overview). Building on this, he exhibited a decomposition of the Hodge locus

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})=\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}\cup\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp}}

into two parts, the typical and atypical part whose distribution is predicted by the:

Conjecture 1.2

(Zilber-Pink conjecture)
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures over a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Then

(a)

the atypical Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp}}
is a strict Zariski-closed subset of
S
;

(b)

if the typical Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}
is non-empty it is dense in
S
for the Zariski topology.

Remark 1.3
.

We will actually work with a refined definition of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}
and
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp}}
due to
[
9
, Def. 2.4, Conj. 2.5]
for pure variations and
[
10
, Def. 4.8, Conj. 4.10]
in general. Part
(a)
of the conjecture was first stated in
[
39
, Conj. 1.10]
and Part
(b)
is an analog of
[
9
, Conj. 2.7]
in the setting of variations of mixed Hodge structures. As we shall show later, it (trivially) cannot hold for variations of mixed Hodge structures with density for the Zariski topology replaced by density for the metric topology as in the case of pure variations.

This work is dedicated to studying part
(b)
of the Zilber-Pink conjecture, partially generalizing results about pure variations in
[
9
,
47
,
31
,
37
,
36
]
to mixed variations. Our first main result is an analog of the all-or-nothing theorem
[
9
, Thm. 3.9]
for pure variations:

Theorem 1.4
.

Assume that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}
is non-empty. Then the full Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
is Zariski-dense in
S
.

In particular, this yields:

Corollary 1.5
.

Conjecture
1.2
(a)
implies Conjecture
1.2
(b)
.

Building on Baldi-Urbanik’s Geometric Zilber-Pink theorem (
[
10
, Thm. 7.1]
), we deduce from Theorem
1.4
the statement of Conjecture
1.2
(ii)
for the typical Hodge locus of factorwise positive dimension (as defined below in Definition
1.20
):

Corollary 1.6
.

The typical Hodge locus of factorwise positive dimension
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ},\mathrm{pos}}
is non-empty if and only if it is Zariski-dense in
S
.

The transverse Hodge locus and the all-or-nothing property

The above described all-or-nothing property follows from an analogous and more precise one for the transverse Hodge locus. To define the latter let us introduce some language for which we refer the non-expert reader to Sect.
2
and
3
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be the generic mixed Hodge datum of
\mathbb{V}
and
\Gamma\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
be an arithmetic lattice containing the image of the monodromy representation of
\mathbb{V}
. For simplicity, we will assume in this introduction that
\Gamma
can be chosen neat. Let
\mathscr{D}=\Gamma\backslash D_{\mathbf{P}}
be the associated Hodge variety and
\Phi:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
the period map of
\mathbb{V}
. For any strict mixed Hodge subdatum
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
we denote by
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})}
the corresponding Hodge subvariety which is a locally closed complex-analytic subset of
\mathscr{D}
.

Definition 1.7

(Definition
4.20
)
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subdatum.

•

A
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
is a complex-analytic irreducible component
Z
of the preimage
\Phi^{-1}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})})
such that:

(a)

\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{an}})
is not contained in the singular locus of
\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
;

(b)

\dim\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{an}})=\dim\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})+\dim\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})}-\dim\mathscr{D}
.

•

The
transverse Hodge locus of type
\mathbf{M}
of
\mathbb{V}
is the union
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
of
(p\mathbf{M}p^{-1},p\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
as
p
ranges over
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
.

•

The transverse Hodge locus of
\mathbb{V}
is the union
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
of all
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})
-transverse special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
over the set of strict mixed Hodge subdata
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

The relevance of these definitions for the study of the typical Hodge locus lies in the following two facts (see Remark
4.21
):

•

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}\subset\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}

•

if Conjecture
1.2
(a)
holds, then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is Zariski-dense in
S
if and only if
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}
is.

Theorem
1.4
is then an immediate consequence of the following property of transverse Hodge loci which is our first main result:

Theorem 1.8

(Theorem
8.1
)
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subdatum. The transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
of type
\mathbf{M}
is non-empty if and only if it is Zariski-dense in
S
.

With this result in hand, a natural goal to pursue is that of understanding when each possibility of the above alternative occurs. The remaining part of this work is dedicated to this problem.

Likeliness and density of the Hodge locus

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subdatum and assume that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty. By definition of transverse special subvarieties, this forces
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
to satisfy the dimension inequality

\dim\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})+\dim\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})}-\dim\mathscr{D}\geqslant 0.

As we shall show, there are actually much more constraints in general: one such inequality for every factor. More precisely, for any normal subgroup
\mathbf{N}
of
\mathbf{P}
whose radical is unipotent, the datum
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
must satisfy

\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})+\dim p_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})})-\dim\mathscr{D}/\mathbf{N}\geqslant 0.

(
\ast_{\mathbf{N}}
)

where
p_{\mathbf{N}}:\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}/\mathbf{N}
is the quotient-by-
\mathbf{N}
(holomorphic surjective) map and
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}=p_{\mathbf{N}}\circ\Phi
.

Definition 1.9
.

A strict mixed Hodge subdatum
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is called
\mathbb{V}
-likely if it satisfies the inequality (

∗

N

) for every normal subgroup
\mathbf{N}
of
\mathbf{P}
whose radical is unipotent.

The distribution of the transverse Hodge locus will depend on the weights that occur in
\mathbb{V}
, hence we will need the following notation:

Notation 1.10
.

Any
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
with non-positive weights only. We will denote by
W_{\bullet}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
the corresponding weight filtration. For integers
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
with
p+q\leqslant 0
, let
h_{\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})}^{p,q}
the Hodge numbers of the weight
p+q
pure Hodge structure on
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}(\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))
defined by
h
. The filtration and Hodge numbers do not depend on
h
hence the omission of
h
in the notation.

Our second main results states that if
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{-2}(\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))=0
, the
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness of a strict mixed Hodge subdatum
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
ensures that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S

for the metric topology
:

Theorem 1.11

(Theorem
8.6
)
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subdatum. Assume that
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{-2}(\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))=0
. The following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty;

(ii)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology;

(iii)

(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.

This in particular includes the analog statement in
[
37
,
31
]
for pure variations and density statements in the metric topology for the torsion locus of some normal functions as
[
36
, Thm. 1.5]
. In general however density for the metric topology may fail for the transverse Hodge locus of some prescribed
\mathbb{V}
-likely type as we demonstrate in an example:

Proposition 1.12

(Proposition
6.2
)
.

There exists a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex curve
C
and an admissible integral graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}
on
C
with generic mixed Hodge datum
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
such that:

(i)

There exists a
\mathbb{V}
-likely strict mixed Hodge subdatum
(\mathbf{M},D_{M})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and for every such mixed Hodge subdatum:

(
i_{1})

the transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(C,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
of type
\mathbf{M}
is dense in
C(\mathbb{C})
for the Zariski topology;

(i_{2})

the transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(C,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
of type
\mathbf{M}
is not dense in
C(\mathbb{C})
for the metric topology;

(ii)

The full transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(C,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
C(\mathbb{C})
for the metric topology.

This result leaves open the question of knowing wether the existence of a
\mathbb{V}
-likely strict mixed Hodge subdatum of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
implies in general that the full transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology. There is one obvious counterexample (see Proposition
6.3
for more details):

Example 1.13

(Proposition
6.3
)
.

Let
S=\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\}
. There is a natural extension
\mathbb{V}
of the constant variation
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{S}(0)
by the constant variation
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{S}(1)
in the category of integral variations of mixed Hodge structures which is admissible. We refer to Sect.
6
for a general description of mixed Hodge-Tate variations, and in particular of their Hodge loci. An easy computation shows that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}=\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})=\mu_{\infty}
where
\mu_{\infty}\subset S^{\mathrm{an}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is the set of roots of unity. In particular it is dense in
S(\mathbb{C})
for the Zariski topology but not for the metric topology.

In general, we can prove:

Theorem 1.14

(Theorem
9.1
)
.

The following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty;

(ii)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S
for the Zariski topology;

(iii)

There exists a
\mathbb{V}
-likely strict mixed Hodge subdatum of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

As we do not know any example other than Example
1.13
where the full transverse Hodge locus fails to be dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology, the following question remains unsolved:

Question 1.15
.

Assume that
h_{\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})}^{-1,-1}\neq 1
and that there exists a
\mathbb{V}
-likely strict mixed Hodge subdatum of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Is
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology?

To finish this section, we highlight the following result in case it could be useful for other purposes:

Proposition 1.16
.

Assume that
W_{-1}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})=W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
. Then the transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
and the transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,(\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})^{\otimes}))_{\mathrm{trans}}
coincide.

Higher level and algebraicity of the Hodge locus

In
[
9
]
, the authors showed that, when the variation is pure, Griffiths’ transversality can force non-trivially the transverse Hodge locus to be empty and could deduce from their geometric Zilber-Pink theorem a finiteness statement for the full Hodge locus in many situations. More precisely, they introduce the following notion:

Definition 1.17

(
[
9
, Def. 4.9, Def. 4.13]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge-Lie algebra
i.e a semi-simple
\mathbb{Q}
-Lie algebra endowed with a
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
0
polarized by the Killing form and such that the Lie bracket is a morphism of Hodge structures
[\cdot,\cdot]:\bigwedge^{2}\mathfrak{h}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}
. The
level of
\mathfrak{h}
is the largest non-negative integer
l\geqslant 0
such that for each
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factor
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{h}
, there exists a
\mathbb{R}
-simple factor
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}_{1}
of the semi-simple
\mathbb{R}
-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}
whose induced Hodge structure has Hodge decomposition:

(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}_{1})_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{-w\leqslant k\leqslant w}(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}_{1})^{k,-k}

with
(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}_{1})^{-w,w}\neq 0
and
w\geqslant l
.

They show:

Theorem 1.18

(
[
9
, Thm. 3.3]
)
.

Assume that
\mathbb{V}
is an integral and polarized variation of Hodge structures on
S
and denote by
\mathbf{H}
its algebraic monodromy group. If the
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge-Lie algebra
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{H})
has level at least
3
the transverse Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is empty.

Remarkably, this is proven independently of the criteria for non-emptiness such as Theorem
1.11
for pure variations that can be found in
[
31
,
37
]
, and the confrontation of the two can yield non-trivial results (see for example
[
38
]
). Motivated by these results, the last part of this work is dedicated to investigating the extent to which the level of the (pure) graded
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
of
\mathbb{V}
for the weight filtration can constrain the transverse Hodge locus of the mixed variation
\mathbb{V}
. For instance, we show (see Theorems
10.2
and
10.3
for a more refined statement):

Theorem 1.19

(Corollary
10.4
, Corollary
10.5
)
.

Let
\mathbf{H}_{0}
(resp.
\mathbf{P}_{0}
) be the algebraic monodromy group (resp. the generic Mumford-Tate group) of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
. Assume that the
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge-Lie algebra
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{H}_{0})
has level at least
3
, that
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
and that one of the following assumptions hold:

(1)

h^{-1,-1}_{\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})}=0

(2)

S
has a smooth compactification
\overline{S}
such that
\overline{S}-S
is either empty, of codimension at least
2
or a smooth irreducible divisor in
\overline{S}
;

Then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is empty.

Let us comment on the assumptions (see Sect.
10
for a more thorough discussion). As illustrated in
[
9
]
through examples, the transverse Hodge locus of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
(hence of
\mathbb{V}
) might be non-empty in levels
1
and
2
, hence the assumption on the level. The other assumptions are meant to exclude the possibility of adding direct factors to
\mathbb{V}
whose monodromy is unipotent, as this doesn’t influence the level of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
while adding factors that are decorrelated to
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
. The assumption
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
is often satisfied (for example when
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-simple and
\mathbb{V}
does not have unipotent monodromy) and the content of the above result is that this assumption is enough to exclude the aforementionned pathological cases, except for some very specific unipotent factors in weight
-2
, studied in length in Sect.
6
and which are excluded by assumptions
(1)
or
(2)
. We do not know if assumptions
(1)
and
(2)
are really needed. Note however that they are of very different natures and make it reasonable to hope that such a result might be applicable in many concrete situations.

The above emptiness criterion yields a finiteness statement for special subvarieties of the following type:

Definition 1.20
.

An irreducible algebraic subvariety
Z\subset S
has
factorwise positive dimension
if for every normal subgroup
\mathbf{N}
of
\mathbf{P}
whose radical is unipotent,

\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(Z^{\mathrm{an}})>0.

The union of special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
which have factorwise positive dimension will be denoted
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{pos}}
.

Indeed, using Baldi-Urbanik’s Geometric Zilber-Pink theorem (
[
10
]
) we prove the following partial generalization of
[
36
, Thm. 3.2]
:

Corollary 1.21

(Theorem
10.9
)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.19
, the Hodge locus of factorwise positive dimension
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{pos}}
is a Zariski-closed subset of
S
.

Remark 1.22
.

Having factorwise positive dimension consists in principle in infinitely many conditions. As we shall explain in Remark
5.13
, Baldi-Urbanik’s theorem implies that all but (non-explicit) finitely many (depending only on
\mathbb{V}
) of these conditions are superfluous for atypical special subvarieties.

Organization of the paper

The cornerstone of the methods used in this paper is Klingler’s realization in
[
39
]
of special subvarieties as intersection loci, and we did not find any detailed account with proofs in the litterature of this realization, in particular no proof of the fact that mixed Mumford-Tate domains parametrize mixed Hodge structures with bounded Mumford-Tate group. In order to be as self-contained as possible we included rather long preliminaries on Mumford-Tate groups of mixed Hodge structures and the associated Mumford-Tate domains, on variations of mixed Hodge structures and on their Hodge loci (Sect.
2
-
4
). We then proceed in Sect.
5
with a quick review of weakly special subvarieties, and two central results in our proofs, namely the Ax-Schanuel and Geometric Zilber-Pink theorems for variations of mixed Hodge structures. These four sections can be safely skipped by experts.

In Sect.
6
, we give an elementary description of families of extensions of
\mathbb{Z}(0)
by
\mathbb{Z}(1)^{n}
(for
n\geqslant 1
) and their Hodge loci. We then exhibit a concrete example realizing Proposition
1.12
. In Sect.
7
we study in depth the
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness condition and prove the core technical results on which our main results rely. In Sect.
8
we prove the all-or-nothing principle of Theorem
1.8
and a more general form of the characterization of density of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
for the metric topology in Theorem
1.11
. In Sect.
9
we prove Theorem
1.14
and discuss Conjecture
1.15
. Finally in Sect.
10
we investigate emptiness of the transverse Hodge locus, algebraicity of the full Hodge locus, and prove generalizations of Theorem
1.19
.

Conventions and notations

•

An algebraic variety is a (non-necessarily irreducible) reduced separated scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
.

•

For a characteristic
0
field
K
and a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
V
(resp. a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathbf{P}
), we denote by
V_{K}
(resp.
\mathbf{P}_{K}
) the object obtained by extension of scalars to
K
. If
V
is a
K
-vector space, we denote by
V^{\vee}
its dual.

•

If
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
A
is an object of an abelian category, endowed with an ascending finite filtration
W_{\bullet}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{k}(A)
its
k
-th graded piece for
W_{\bullet}
and by
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}(A)
the coproduct of the non-trivial graded-pieces of
A
for
W_{\bullet}
. For mixed Hodge structures and variations of mixed Hodge structures, when the superscript indicating the filtration is omitted, it should be understood that we are considering the graded for the weight filtration.

•

\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups will be denoted in bold letters (
\mathbf{P}
,
\mathbf{H}
,
\mathbf{G}
etc.). For a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathbf{P}
, we denote by
\mathbf{P}^{u}
its unipotent radical, by
Z(\mathbf{P})
its center, by
\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{ad}}
its adjoint and by
\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{der}}
its derived subgroup. We also denote by
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
the connected component of the identity of the real Lie group
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})
and by
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
the intersection
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\cap\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})
. A (
\mathbb{Q}
-)representation of
\mathbf{P}
will mean a rational, algebraic and finite-dimensional representation of
\mathbf{P}
. If
(V,q)
is a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space endowed with a non-degenerate bilinear form, we denote by
\mathbf{GAut}(V,q)
the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group of linear automorphisms of
V
preserving
q
up to scaling.

Aknowledgements

I thank my advisor Emmanuel Ullmo for suggesting this project and for many enlightening discussions and suggestions which greatly helped to improve this work. I also thank Ziyang Gao for a useful discussion that led to Lemma
2.15
.

Mixed Hodge structures and their parameter spaces

Mixed Hodge structures

First definitions

Definition 2.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. A
pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
n
is the datum of a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
V
and of a decreasing finite filtration by
\mathbb{C}
-vector subspaces
F^{\bullet}
on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, called the
Hodge filtration
, such that
V_{\mathbb{C}}=F^{p}\oplus\overline{F^{n+1-p}}
for all
p\in\mathbb{Z}
. A
pure
\mathbb{Z}
-Hodge structure of weight
n
is the datum of a torsion-free abelian group of finite rank
V
and of a pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
n
on
V_{\mathbb{Q}}=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space, let
\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{Res}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}})
be the Deligne torus which is a real algebraic torus split over
\mathbb{C}
and
w:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
be the weight homomorphism defined on
\mathbb{R}
-points as the inclusion
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\subset\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The datum of a pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
n
on
V
is equivalent to that of

1.

a bigraduation
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p+q=n}V^{p,q}
called the
Hodge decomposition
subject to the
Hodge symmetry
condition
V^{p,q}=\overline{V^{q,p}}
for the complex conjugation on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
defined by the real form
V_{\mathbb{R}}
. The set
\{(p,q)\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544ptV^{p,q}\neq 0\}\subset\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
is called the
type
of the Hodge structure.

2.

a morphism of real algebraic groups
x:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{R}}
, called the
Hodge homomorphism
such that
x\circ w
is given on real points by
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\ni r\mapsto r^{n}\cdot\mathrm{id}\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathbb{R}})
.

Example 2.2
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}
. There is a unique pure
\mathbb{Z}
(resp.
\mathbb{Q}
)-Hodge structure of weight
-2r
on
V=(2i\pi)^{r}\mathbb{Z}
(resp
(2i\pi)^{r}\mathbb{Q}
) called the
Tate Hodge structure
and it is denoted by
\mathbb{Z}(r)
(resp.
\mathbb{Q}(r)
). It is of type
\{(-r,-r)\}
.

Example 2.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth compact Kähler variety. For any
0\leqslant n\leqslant 2\dim X
, the
n
-th cohomology group modulo torsion
H^{n}(X,\mathbb{Z})
carries a canonical
\mathbb{Z}
-Hodge structure of weight
k
.

Among compact Kähler varieties those which are actually smooth projective complex algebraic varieties are privileged, and characterized by the property that they admit a Kähler class lying in
H^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. By Hodge index theorem, this additional piece of linear algebra data is the notion of polarization which we recall now.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
V=(V,F^{\bullet})
be a pure
\mathbb{Q}
(resp.
\mathbb{Z}
)-Hodge structure of weight
n
, let
x:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathbb{R}})
be the associated Hodge homomorphism and
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p+q=n}V^{p,q}
the corresponding Hodge decomposition. Let
C=x(i)
. A
polarization of
V
is a non-degenerate bilinear
(-1)^{n}
-symmetric form
\psi:V\times V\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
(resp.
\psi:V\times V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
) such that:

•

for every
p+q=n
and
r+s=n
such that
(p,q)\neq(r,s)
,

\psi_{\mathbb{C}}(V^{p,q},\overline{V^{r,s}})=0;

(2.1.1)

•

denoting by
h=i^{n}\psi_{\mathbb{C}}(\cdot,\overline{\cdot})
the associated hermitian form on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, for every
p+q=n
and
v\in V^{p,q}
,

i^{p-q}h(v,v)>0.

(2.1.2)

In other words
h(\cdot,C(\cdot))
is a hermitian form which makes the Hodge decomposition orthogonal and is positive definite on the
V^{p,q}
’s.

Example 2.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth complex projective algebraic variety. For any
0\leqslant n\leqslant 2\dim X
, the
n
-th primitive cohomology group modulo torsion
H^{n}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{prim}}
carries a canonical pure
polarized

\mathbb{Z}
-Hodge structure of weight
n

By the celebrated work of Deligne
[
25
,
28
]
, the existence of a remarkable linear algebraic structure on the cohomology of smooth projective varieties extends to possibly open and singular varieties, at the cost of allowing more general structures, which roughly arise as successive extensions of pure Hodge structures of various weights.

Definition 2.6
.

(a)

A
mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
is the datum of a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
V
, of an increasing finite filtration by
\mathbb{Q}
-vector subspaces
W_{\bullet}
on
V
, called the
weight filtration
, and of a decreasing finite filtration by
\mathbb{C}
-vector subspaces
F^{\bullet}
on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, called
the Hodge filtration
, such that for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the induced filtration
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{n}(F^{\bullet})
defines a pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
n
on
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{n}(V)
.

(b)

A
graded-polarization of a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure

(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
is the datum for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
of a form
q_{n}
on
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{n}(V)
which is a polarization of the pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
n
on
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{n}(V)
induced by
F^{\bullet}
.

(c)

A
mixed
\mathbb{Z}
-Hodge structure
is the datum of torsion-free abelian group of finite rank
V
and of a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V_{\mathbb{Q}}=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

(d)

A
graded polarization of a mixed
\mathbb{Z}
-Hodge structure
is a graded-polarization of the corresponding mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure.

Example 2.7
.

Let
X
be a smooth complex algebraic variety. For any
0\leqslant n\leqslant 2\dim X
the
n
-th cohomology space
H^{n}(X,\mathbb{Q})
of
X
is endowed in
[
25
]
with a canonical mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure with weights between
n
and
2n
.

Example 2.8
.

Let
X
be a (possibly singular) complex proper algebraic variety. For any
0\leqslant n\leqslant 2\dim X
the
n
-th cohomology space
H^{n}(X,\mathbb{Q})
of
X
is endowed in
[
28
]
with a canonical mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure with weights between
0
and
n
.

Let
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure. For
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
(p,q)
-
Hodge number
of
V
is the integer

h^{p,q}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}F^{p}(\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}V)_{\mathbb{C}}/F^{p+1}(\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}V)_{\mathbb{C}}.

Lemma 2.9
.

For every
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, one has
h^{p,q}=h^{q,p}
.

Proof.

Let
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then
F^{\bullet}
induces a pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of weight
p+q
on
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}V
and it is easily seen that
h^{p,q}
is the dimension of the
(p,q)
-piece of the Hodge decomposition of
(\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}V)_{\mathbb{C}}
, which satisfies Hodge symmetry by definition. The result follows.
∎

Bigraduations

By analogy with the pure case, it is natural to try to encode the datum of a mixed Hodge structure in that of a bigraduation as follows:

Definition 2.10
.

Let
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure. A
bigraduation of
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
is a direct sum decomposition
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{p,q}
such that:

•

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(W_{k}V)_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p+q\leqslant k}V^{p,q};

(2.1.3)

•

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,

F^{r}V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p\geqslant r}V^{p,q};

(2.1.4)

Conversely, a decomposition
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{p,q}
is said to
define a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
if the filtrations defined by (
2.1.4
) and (
2.1.3
) define a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure.

Remark 2.11
.

Let
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{p,q}
be a bigraduation of some mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure with Hodge numbers
h^{p,q}
. From (
2.1.3
) and (
2.1.4
), there exists a
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphism
V^{p,q}\rightarrow F^{p}(\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}V)_{\mathbb{C}}/F^{p+1}(\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{p+q}V)_{\mathbb{C}}
. In particular
\dim V^{p,q}=h^{p,q}
.

In general, there is no unique way to define a bigraduation of a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure. However, one still has:

Proposition 2.12

(
[
25
, Prop. 1.2.8]
,
[
32
, Prop. 2.2]
)
.

Let
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure. There exists a unique bigraduation
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}V^{p,q}
of
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
such that for each
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
,

V^{p,q}=\overline{V^{q,p}}\mod\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}r<p\\
s<q\end{subarray}}V^{r,s}.

(2.1.5)

This bigraduation will be referred to as the canonical bigraduation of
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
.

As the datum of a bigraduation of the
\mathbb{C}
-vector space
V_{\mathbb{C}}
is equivalent to that of a morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-groups
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
, the canonical bigraduation associates such a morphism
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
to any mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V
. Conversely, Pink proved the following which elucidates under which conditions such a morphism actually defines a polarizable mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V
.

Lemma 2.13

(
[
43
, (1.4) and (1.5)]
)
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space. A morphism
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure if and only if there exists a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathbf{P}
and a
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
such that
x
factors through
\rho_{\mathbb{C}}
and the following hold:

(i)

the composite
\bar{x}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u})_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
;

(ii)

the composite
\bar{x}\circ w:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u})_{\mathbb{R}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
and factors through the center of
\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u}
;

(iii)

the weight filtration on
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}
defined by
\mathrm{ad}_{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}}\circ x
through formula (
2.1.3
) satisfies
W_{0}(\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}})=\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
W_{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}})=\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Under this assumption, the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V
defined by
x
is polarizable if and only if for a group
\mathbf{P}
as above the following holds:

(iv)

the involution
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}_{\mathbb{R}}(i))
is a Cartan involution of
(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{ad}}

Under both assumptions, denoting by
W_{\bullet}
the weight filtration on
V
defined by
x
, by
(q_{k})
some graded-polarization of the latter mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure and by
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
the
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
whose
R
-points for any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
are

\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}(R)=\{g\in\mathbf{GL}(V_{R})\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544ptg(W_{k})\subset W_{k}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{and}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{W}(g)\in\mathbf{GAut}(\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{W}(V),q_{k})(R)\hskip 2.84544pt\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}\},

the morphism
x
satisfies properties
(i)-(iv)
for
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
, with
\rho
being the natural inclusion in
\mathbf{GL}(V)
. Conversely, any pair
(\mathbf{P},\rho)
for which
x
satisfies properties
(i)-(iv)
satisfies that
\rho(\mathbf{P})
is contained in
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
for some graded-polarization
(q_{k})
of
V
.

Proof.

The first assertion is
[
43
, (1.5)]
. The second is
[
43
, (1.12)]
with the "
G_{1}
" in
op. cit.
equal to
(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u})^{\mathrm{ad}}
. The third assertion is a byproduct of the proof of
[
43
, (1.5)]
combined with the definition of graded-polarizations. The fourth assertion is
[
43
, (1.4)(b)]
combined with the definition of graded-polarizations.
∎

Definition 2.14
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space and
\mathbf{P}
a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
. A morphism
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
is said to
satisfy Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
if
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
and the resulting morphism
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfies properties
(i)-(iv)
of Lemma
2.13
.

We will need the following slight refinement of Lemma
2.13
, which gives more informations on the groups for which a bigraduation of a graded-polarized mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure satisfies Pink’s axioms. Its statement and proof were communicated to me by Z. Gao (but any mistake is of course mine).

Lemma 2.15

(Gao)
.

Let
V
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure with weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
and graded-polarized by
(q_{k})
. Let
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
be some bigraduation of
V
. Let
\mathbf{P}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
such that
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
. Then
x
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
. Furthermore
\mathbf{P}^{u}=(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})^{u}\cap\mathbf{P}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{P}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
such that
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
. As the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V
is polarizable, the third assertion of Lemma
2.13
implies that the morphism
x
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
. We first prove that
\mathbf{P}^{u}=(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})^{u}\cap\mathbf{P}
. The inclusion of the right-hand side in the left-hand side is immediate. For the other inclusion, we need to prove that
\mathbf{G}:=\mathbf{P}/((\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})^{u}\cap\mathbf{P})
is reductive. Denote by
\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
the quotient of
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
by its unipotent radical, so that the inclusion of
\mathbf{P}
in
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
descends to an inclusion of
\mathbf{G}
in
\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
. Since
x
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
, the composite morphism
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
, and we denote it by
\bar{x}:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{R}}
. Again by Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
, the involution
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}(i))
is a Cartan involution of
(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Because
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
, the morphism
\bar{x}
factors through the inclusion of
\mathbf{G}
in
\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
, hence the involution
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}(i))
on
(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
restricts to an involution of
(\mathbf{G}/(Z(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})\cap\mathbf{G}))_{\mathbb{R}}
, which is a Cartan involution because the real form it defines is a closed subgroup of the real form of
(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
defined by
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}(i))
, which is compact since
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}(i))
is a Cartan involution of
(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Therefore
(\mathbf{G}/(Z(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})\cap\mathbf{G})
is reductive hence so is
\mathbf{G}
as
Z(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})\cap\mathbf{G}\subset Z(\mathbf{G})
. Thus we have shown that
\mathbf{P}^{u}=(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})^{u}\cap\mathbf{P}
. In particular, letting
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{P}}=\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u}
the reductive quotient of
\mathbf{P}
, this implies that the inclusion of
\mathbf{P}
in
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
descends to an inclusion of
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{P}}
in
\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
. It is easy and left to the reader to see that the latter fact combined to assertions
(i)-(iii)
of Pink’s axioms for
x
and
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
implies that
x
satisfies assertions
(i)-(iii)
of Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
. For
(iv)
, once we know that
\mathbf{P}^{u}=(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})^{u}\cap\mathbf{P}
, repeating the first part of the proof shows that
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}(i))
is a Cartan involution of
(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{P}}/(Z(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})\cap\mathbf{G}))_{\mathbb{R}}
. As
Z(\mathbf{G}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})})\cap\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}\subset Z(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}
, it follows that
\mathrm{ad}(\bar{x}(i))
is a Cartan involution of
(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{P}})^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{R}}
. The result is proven.

∎

Canonical bigraduations of tensor products and duals

The end of this subsection is dedicated to describing the canonical bigraduations associated to the natural mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures on tensor products and duals of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces endowed with mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures. This will be crucial for the characterization of Mumford-Tate groups given in Proposition
2.21
.

Let
V
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-mixed Hodge structure and
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
be the canonical bigraduation of
V
. Consider the morphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups
\phi:\mathbf{GL}(V)\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V^{\vee})
defined on
R
-points for
R
a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra by sending
g\in\mathbf{GL}(V_{R})
to the
R
-linear automorphism of
V_{R}^{\vee}
mapping a
R
-linear form
f
to
f\circ g^{-1}
. Set
x^{\vee}=\phi_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x
.

Lemma 2.16
.

The morphism
x^{\vee}
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V^{\vee}
. Furthermore, the canonical bigraduation of
V^{\vee}
endowed with this mixed Hodge structure is
x^{\vee}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.13
, the morphism
x
factors through the complexification of a
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
of a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathbf{P}
such that properties
(i)-(iii)
of the lemma are satisfied. By construction,
x^{\vee}
then factors through the complexification of the
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\phi\circ\rho
of
\mathbf{P}
and conditions
(i)-(iii)
are automatically satisfied as they are relative to the morphism
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
which is the same as for
x
. By Lemma
2.13
this shows that
x^{\vee}
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V^{\vee}
.

It remains to see that
x^{\vee}
is the canonical bigraduation of
V^{\vee}
for the above mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure, i.e. to check condition (
2.1.5
) of Proposition
2.12
. We start by expliciting the bigraduation defined on
V^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}
by
x^{\vee}
. To lighten the notation, we set for
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

(V^{p,q})^{\vee}:=\Big(V/{\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(p,q)}V^{r,s}}\Big)^{\vee}

which is canonically a sub-
\mathbb{C}
-vector space of
V_{\mathbb{C}}^{\vee}
. Then, one easily checks that the bigraduation on
V_{\mathbb{C}}^{\vee}
defined by
x^{\vee}
is:

(V^{\vee})^{p,q}=(V^{-p,-q})^{\vee},

for
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, and that this bigraduation indeed gives the weight and Hodge filtrations on the dual that appear in the litterature (see e.g.
[
25
, Sect. 1.1]
:

W_{l}V^{\vee}=\Big(V/W_{-l-1}\Big)^{\vee}

and

F^{r}V_{\mathbb{C}}^{\vee}=\Big(V_{\mathbb{C}}/F^{1-r}V_{\mathbb{C}}\Big)^{\vee}.

Let
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, set
R^{p,q}=\bigoplus_{r<p,s<q}(V^{\vee})^{r,s}
and
\pi_{p,q}:V^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow V^{\vee}_{\mathbb{C}}/R^{p,q}
be the quotient map. We wish to prove that
\pi_{p,q}((V^{\vee})^{p,q})=\pi_{p,q}(\overline{(V^{\vee})^{q,p}})
. We start by giving an explicit description of
\pi_{p,q}
. First, one has

R^{p,q}=\bigoplus_{r<p,s<q}\Big(\frac{V_{\mathbb{C}}}{\bigoplus_{(k,l)\neq(-r,-s)}V^{k,l}}\Big)^{\vee}=\Big(\frac{V_{\mathbb{C}}}{\bigcap_{r<p,s<q}\bigoplus_{(k,l)\neq(-r,-s)}V^{k,l}}\Big)^{\vee}=\Big(\frac{V_{\mathbb{C}}}{\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}}\Big)^{\vee}

where
I_{p,q}=\{(r,s)\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544ptr\leqslant-p\}\cup\{(r,s)\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544pts\leqslant-q\}
.
Then, the quotient vector space
V_{\mathbb{C}}^{\vee}/R^{p,q}
is canonically isomorphic to
\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big)^{\vee}
, and under this identification,
\pi_{p,q}
identifies to the map which sends a
\mathbb{C}
-linear form on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
to its restriction to the
\mathbb{C}
-vector subspace
\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}
.

Besides, in view of the definition of the
(V^{\vee})^{p,q}
’s, a
\mathbb{C}
-linear form is in
(V^{\vee})^{p,q}
(resp.
\overline{(V^{\vee})^{q,p}}
) if and only if
\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}V^{r,s}\subset\ker f
(resp.
\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-q,-p)}\overline{V^{r,s}}\subset\ker f
). It follows that proving that
\pi_{p,q}((V^{\vee})^{p,q})=\pi_{p,q}(\overline{(V^{\vee})^{q,p}})
reduces to the equality of
\mathbb{C}
-vector subspaces

\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}V^{r,s}\Big)\cap\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big)=\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}\overline{V^{s,r}}\Big)\cap\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big).

Let’s first prove that both have the same dimension. First note that for
r,s\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}V^{r,s}=h^{r,s}=h^{s,r}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}V^{s,r}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{V^{s,r}}
. Therefore,

\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}V^{r,s}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}\overline{V^{s,r}}.

Besides, since
(-p,-q)\in I^{p,q}
, one has

\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}V^{r,s}\Big)+\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big)=V_{\mathbb{C}}.

Finally (
2.1.5
) of Proposition
2.12
implies that
\overline{V^{-q,-p}}\subset V^{-p,-q}\oplus\bigoplus_{r<-p,s<-q}V^{r,s}\subset\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}
which shows that

\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}\overline{V^{s,r}}\Big)+\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big)=V_{\mathbb{C}}.

By the Grassman formula, this implies that the
\mathbb{C}
-dimensions of the above two intersections are equal. Therefore, we are reduced to proving one inclusion. Again by (
2.1.5
) of Proposition
2.12
, we have

\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}-\{(-p,-q)\}}\overline{V^{s,r}}\subset\sum_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}-\{(-p,-q)\}}\Big[V^{r,s}\oplus\bigoplus_{k<r,l<s}V^{k,l}\Big]\subset\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}-\{(-p,-q)\}}V^{r,s}.

Since the first and last member of this chain of inclusions of
\mathbb{C}
-vector subspaces of
V_{\mathbb{C}}
have the same dimension, all inclusions are equalities. Therefore, one finds that

\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}-\{(-p,-q)\}}V^{r,s}=\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}-\{(-p,-q)\}}\overline{V^{s,r}}\subset\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}\overline{V^{s,r}}\Big)\cap\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big)

and the desired inclusion follows from the remark that:

\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\neq(-p,-q)}V^{r,s}\Big)\cap\Big(\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I_{p,q}}V^{r,s}\Big)=\bigoplus_{(r,s)\in I^{p,q}-\{(-p,-q)\}}V^{r,s}.

This finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
be two mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures and
x_{i}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{i,\mathbb{C}})

(i=1,2)
their respective canonical bigraduations. Consider the map of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups
\psi:\mathbf{GL}(V_{1})\times\mathbf{GL}(V_{2})\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})
which for any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra sends a pair of
R
-linear automorphisms
(g_{1},g_{2})
to the
R
-linear automorphism of
V_{1,R}\otimes_{R}V_{2,R}
defined on pure tensors by
v_{1}\otimes v_{2}\mapsto g_{1}(v_{1})\otimes g_{2}(v_{2})
. Denote by
x_{1}\otimes x_{2}
the morphism
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})
defined as
\psi_{\mathbb{C}}\circ(x_{1},x_{2})
where
(x_{1},x_{2})
is the product morphism
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{1,\mathbb{C}})\times\mathbf{GL}(V_{2,\mathbb{C}})
defined in the usual categorical way.

Lemma 2.17
.

The morphism
x_{1}\otimes x_{2}
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V_{1}\otimes V_{2}
. Furthermore, the canonical bigraduation of
V_{1}\otimes V_{2}
endowed with this mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure is
x_{1}\otimes x_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. By Lemma
2.13
, there is a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathbf{P}_{i}
and a representation
\rho_{i}:\mathbf{P}_{i}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{i})
such that
x_{i}
factors by
\rho_{i,\mathbb{C}}
and satisfies axioms
(i)-(iii)
of the lemma. Then,
(x_{1},x_{2})
factors through the complexification of the
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\psi\circ(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
of
\mathbf{P}_{1}\times\mathbf{P}_{2}
in
\mathbf{GL}(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})
. It is easily checked that
(\mathbf{P}_{1}\times\mathbf{P}_{2})^{u}=(\mathbf{P}_{1})^{u}\times(\mathbf{P}_{2})^{u}
and that under the canonical identification
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{1}\times\mathbf{P}_{2})\cong\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{1})\oplus\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{2})
, the weight filtration on the former, induced by post-composition with the adjoint representation of
\mathbf{P}_{1}\times\mathbf{P}_{2}
is given by

W_{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{1}\times\mathbf{P}_{2})\cong W_{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{1})\oplus W_{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{2})

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. It follows that
(x_{1},x_{2})=\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{1}\times\mathbf{P}_{2}
satisfies axioms
(i)-(iii)
of Lemma
2.13
which proves the first part of the statement.

It remains to see that
\psi_{\mathbb{C}}\circ(x_{1},x_{2})
is indeed the canonical bigraduation of the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
V_{1}\otimes V_{2}
that it defines. Explicitely, the bigraduation
\psi_{\mathbb{C}}\circ(x_{1},x_{2})
of
V_{1,\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{2,\mathbb{C}}
is

(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{p,q}:=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q\end{subarray}}V_{1}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\otimes V_{2}^{p_{2},q_{2}}

for
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, where we denoted by
V_{i}^{p,q}
the pieces of the bigraduation
x_{i}
of
V_{i,\mathbb{C}}

(i=0,1)
. We wish to prove condition (
2.1.5
) of Proposition
2.12
, that is for any
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{p,q}=\overline{(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{q,p}}\mod\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}r<p\\
s<q\end{subarray}}(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{r,s}.

To see this, remark that

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}r<p\\
s<q\end{subarray}}(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{r,s}\supseteq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q\end{subarray}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1}<p_{1},r_{2}<p_{2}\\
s_{1}<q_{1},s_{2}<q_{2}\end{subarray}}V_{1}^{r_{1},s_{1}}\otimes V_{2}^{r_{2},s_{2}}

so that it suffices to prove the equality of
(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{p,q}
and
\overline{(V_{1}\otimes V_{2})^{q,p}}
modulo the right hand side of the last equation. But this equality is now an immediate consequence of condition (
2.1.5
) of Proposition
2.12
for
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

An easy induction combining Lemmata
2.16
and
2.17
easily implies the following:

Corollary 2.18
.

Let
V
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure, and
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
be the canonical bigraduation of
V
. Let
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{2}-\{(0,0)\}
and
x_{a,b}=x\otimes\cdots\otimes x\otimes x^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes x^{\vee}
where
x
appears
a
times and
x^{\vee}
appears
b
times. The morphism
x_{a,b}
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
T^{a,b}(V):=V^{\otimes a}\otimes(V^{\vee})^{\otimes b}
and the canonical bigraduation of the latter is
x_{a,b}
.

Mumford-Tate groups

Let
V
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure.

Definition 2.19
.

A
Hodge tensor for
V
is an element of
F^{0}(T^{a,b}(V_{\mathbb{C}}))\cap W_{0}(T^{a,b}(V))
for some
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{2}-\{(0,0)\}
. We denote by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(V^{\otimes})
the bigraded (by the bidegree) vector space of Hodge tensors for
V
.

We then take the following as a definition of Mumford-Tate group, which is equivalent to the usual tannakian definition by classical arguments (see for example
[
1
]
).

Definition 2.20
.

The Mumford-Tate group
\mathbf{P}(V)
of
V
is the subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
fixing all Hodge tensors for
V
.

We have the following characterisation, which will be crucial for the interpretation of Hodge loci as intersection loci and which makes the link with the definition below
[
32
, Prop. 2.3]
:

Proposition 2.21
.

Let
V
be a polarizable mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure. For any bigraduation
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
of
V
, denote by
\mathbf{P}(x)
the intersection of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
whose
\mathbb{C}
-points contain
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})
.

(i)

If
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
is any bigraduation of
V
, one has
\mathbf{P}(V)\subset\mathbf{P}(x)
;

(ii)

If
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
is the canonical bigraduation of
V
one has
\mathbf{P}(V)=\mathbf{P}(x)
.

Proof.

We will use the following two lemmas to be proven below.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
M
be a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure. Let

M_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p,q}M^{p,q}

be a bigraduation of
M
. Then
M^{0,0}\cap M\subset W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})
. If furthermore the bigraduation is the canonical bigraduation, we have equality
W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})=M^{0,0}\cap M
.

Lemma 2.23
.

Let
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
be any bigraduation of
V
, and let
\mathbf{P}(x)^{\prime}
be the largest
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
which fixes pointwise all rational tensors of
V
which are fixed by
\mathbf{P}(x)
. Then
\mathbf{P}(x)=\mathbf{P}(x^{\prime})
.

(i)
Let
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
be any bigraduation of
V
. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geqslant 0}-\{(0,0)\}
. Let
\zeta\in T^{a,b}(V)
be a rational tensor which is fixed by
\mathbf{P}(x)
. It is henceforth fixed by
x_{a,b}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})
, or equivalently is an eigentensor for the trivial character of
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}
. It follows that
\zeta
belongs to
T^{a,b}(V)^{0,0}\cap T^{a,b}(V)
. Applying the first assertion of Lemma
2.22
to the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
T^{a,b}(V)
and the bigraduation
x_{a,b}
, we find that any
\zeta
is a Hodge tensor, hence is fixed by
\mathbf{P}(V)
. Therefore
\mathbf{P}(V)\subset\mathbf{P}(x)^{\prime}
. Now by Lemma
2.23
, we have
\mathbf{P}(x)=\mathbf{P}(x)^{\prime}
, and the desired inclusion
\mathbf{P}(V)\subset\mathbf{P}(x)
follows.

(ii)
Assume futhermore that
x
is the canonical bigraduation of
V
. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geqslant 0}-\{(0,0)\}
and
\zeta\in T^{a,b}(V)
be a Hodge tensor. By Corollary
2.18
the morphism
x_{a,b}
is the canonical bigraduation of
T^{a,b}
and applying Lemma
2.22
(ii) to the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
T^{a,b}(V)
and its canonical bigraduation
x_{a,b}
we find that
\zeta\in T^{a,b}(V)^{0,0}\cap T^{a,b}(V)
. Therefore
\zeta
is fixed by
x_{a,b}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})
. Since this is true for an arbitrary Hodge tensor, one finds that
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
hence
\mathbf{P}(x)\subset\mathbf{P}(V)
by minimality of
\mathbf{P}(x)
, as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.22
.

The inclusion
M^{0,0}\cap M\subset W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})
is an immediate consequence of the definition of a bigraduation of a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure, since it forces
M^{0,0}
to lie simultaneously in
W_{0}(M)_{\mathbb{C}}
and
F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})
.

We now assume that the given bigraduation is the canonical bigraduation and seek to prove the inverse inclusion. We need to prove that for any
(p,q)\neq(0,0)
, we have that
\Big(W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})\Big)\cap M^{p,q}=\{0\}
. We assume this does not hold, and pick some pair of indexes
(p,q)\neq(0,0)
such that

\Big(W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})\Big)\cap M^{p,q}\neq\{0\}

First remark that by equations (
2.1.3
) and (
2.1.4
) of Proposition
2.12
, the indexes have to satisfy
p+q\leqslant 0
and
p\geqslant 0
. Because
(p,q)\neq(0,0)
, this forces
q<0
. Furthermore, since
W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})
consists of rational vectors, one must have by taking complex conjugation of the above identity

\Big(W_{0}(M)\cap F^{0}(M_{\mathbb{C}})\Big)\cap\overline{M^{p,q}}\neq\{0\}.

By equation (
2.1.5
) of Proposition
2.12
, we find that

\overline{M^{p,q}}\subset M^{q,p}\oplus\bigoplus_{r<q,s<p}M^{r,s}.

But we saw that
q<0
so that the right hand side intersects
F^{0}M_{\mathbb{C}}
in
\{0\}
. This gives the desired contradiction
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.23
.

We adapt the proof of
[
1
, Lem. 2.(a)]
, replacing the fact that the underlying space of any representation of
\mathbf{P}(V)
is canonically endowed with a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure, by the fact that the underlying space of any rational representation of
\mathbf{P}(x)
is endowed with a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure obtained by precomposing the complexified representation with the cocharacter
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}(x)_{\mathbb{C}}
. The inclusion
\mathbf{P}(x)\subset\mathbf{P}(x)^{\prime}
is true by definition. To prove the other inclusion, it suffices to prove that there exists a finite set of pairs of indexes
(m_{i},n_{i})
, a sub-
\mathbf{GL}(V)
-module
W
of
\bigoplus_{i}T^{m_{i},n_{i}}(V)
and a rational vector
w\in W
such that
\mathbf{P}(x)
is the biggest subgroup
\mathbf{Fix}_{\mathbf{GL}(V)}(w)
of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
which fixes
w
. Indeed assume the latter statement. Then the components
w_{i}
of
w
are rational tensors of
V
which are fixed by
\mathbf{P}(x)
. Therefore
\mathbf{P}(x)^{\prime}\subset\bigcap_{i}\mathbf{Fix}_{\mathbf{GL}(V)}(w_{i})
. Since the latter was assumed to be precisely
\mathbf{P}(x)
, this gives the desired inclusion.

By
[
29
, Prop. 3.1(a)-(b))]
, there exists a finite set of pairs of indexes
(m_{i},n_{i})
, a sub-
\mathbf{GL}(V)
-module
W
of
\bigoplus_{i}T^{m_{i},n_{i}}(V)
and a rational line
L\subset W
such that
\mathbf{P}(x)=\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathbf{GL}(V)}(L)
. By the third assertion of Lemma
2.13
one has
\mathbf{P}(x)\subset\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
for some graded-polarization
(q_{k})
of
V
. Hence, by Lemma
2.15
the morphism
x
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}(x)
. Therefore post-composition of
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}(x)_{\mathbb{C}}
with the complexification of the induced representation
\mathbf{P}(x)\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(L)
makes
L
a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge sub-structure of some
\bigoplus_{i}T^{m_{i},n_{i}}(V)
. From here, one finishes the proof as in
[
1
, Lem. 2(a)]
, which we recall for completeness. Since it has rank one
L
must be isomorphic to some Tate Hodge structure
\mathbb{Q}(N_{1})
.

If
N_{1}=0
, then
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})
acts trivially on
L_{\mathbb{C}}
and because
\mathbf{P}(x)
is the smallest
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
such that
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}(x)_{\mathbb{C}}
, it must also act trivially on
L
. Combined with
\mathbf{P}(x)=\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathbf{GL}(V)}(L)
, this forces
\mathbf{P}(x)=\mathbf{Fix}_{\mathbf{GL}(V)}(w)
for any generator
w
of
L
.

If
N_{1}\neq 0
then
V
must have non-zero weights, because otherwise all the
T^{m,n}(V)
would be pure of weight
0
and so would be the sub-structure
L
. As a consequence, there exists an integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\det(W_{n}(V))
has non-zero weight
N_{2}
. Up to replacing
V
by
V^{\vee}
, one can assume that
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
have the same sign. Let
r=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{n}(V)
. Then, the rank one mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge sub-structure

L^{\otimes|N_{2}|}\otimes(\bigwedge^{r}W_{n}(V))^{\otimes 2|N_{1}|}

of

(\bigoplus_{i}T^{m_{i},n_{i}})^{\otimes|N_{2}|}\otimes(\bigwedge^{r}V)^{\otimes 2|N_{1}|}

has weight
0
hence isomorphic to
\mathbb{Q}(0)
. Arguing as in the
N_{1}=0
case, one finds that
\mathbf{P}(x)
is the fixator in
\mathbf{GL}(V)
(acting on
(\bigoplus_{i}T^{m_{i},n_{i}})^{\otimes|N_{2}|}\otimes(\wedge^{r}V)^{\otimes 2|N_{1}|}
) of any generator of the line
L^{\otimes|N_{2}|}\otimes(\bigwedge^{r}W_{n}(V))^{\otimes 2|N_{1}|}
.

In both case a representation is constructed that satisfies the asked for properties at the beginning of the proof.
∎

Mixed Hodge classes

In this subsection, following
[
39
]
, we recall the formalism of mixed Hodge data, and introduce the notion of mixed Hodge class which gives a nice functorial setting to work with parameter spaces of mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures with prescribed Mumford-Tate group. The main goal, building on the previous two subsections, is a proof that connected mixed Mumford-Tate domains indeed parametrize mixed Hodge structures with prescribed Mumford-Tate group (Proposition
2.37
).

Mixed Hodge data

The starting point is the following particular case (for faithful representations) of
[
43
, (1.7)]
whose statement is
[
39
, Prop. 3.1]
:

Proposition 2.24

(
[
43
, (1.7)]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{P}
be a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group, let
x_{0}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
be a morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-algebraic groups satisfying the axioms
(i)-(iv)
of Lemma
2.13
, and let
X_{\mathbf{P}}
be the
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
x_{0}
in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})
, where
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{C})
acts by conjugation on the target. Let
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
be a faithful and finite-dimensional representation of
\mathbf{P}
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group. Then,

(i)

any
x\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
satisfies the axioms
(i)-(iv)
of Lemma
2.13
;

(ii)

the image
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P},\rho}
of the map (well defined by
(i)
)

\psi_{\rho,X_{\mathbf{P}}}:X_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\{\mathrm{mixed}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbb{Q}\mathrm{-Hodge}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{structures}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{on}\hskip 2.84544ptV\}

admits a unique structure of complex manifold such that the Hodge filtration on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
corresponding to
\psi_{\rho,X_{\mathbf{P}}}(x)
for
x\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
varies holomorphically with
\psi_{\rho,X_{\mathbf{P}}}(x)
. The action of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
on
X_{\mathbf{P}}
descends to
D_{\mathbf{P},\rho}
along
\psi_{\rho,X_{\mathbf{P}}}
. The complex structure on
D_{\mathbf{P},\rho}
is invariant under this action of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
acts holomorphically on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P},\rho}
;

(iii)

The complex manifold
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P},\rho}
is independent of the choice of the faithful representation
\rho
, and will be denoted
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
;

(iv)

For this complex manifold structure, there is an open embedding into a flag manifold
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}\hookrightarrow\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}:=\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{C})/\exp(F^{0}\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}})
, where
\mathfrak{p}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
endowed with the adjoint mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure induced by
x_{0}
. This embedding realizes
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
as a
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}

Proof.

The only point which is not in
[
43
, (1.7)]
is the fact that axiom
(iv)
of Lemma
2.13
is preserved under
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-conjugation of morphisms
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
on the target. This is contained in
[
43
, (1.12)]
.
∎

Remark 2.25
.

In view of point
(iii)
, there exists a unique map
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}:X_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
such that for any faithful representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
, there exists an injective holomorphic map

j_{\rho}:\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\{\mathrm{mixed}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbb{Q}\mathrm{-Hodge}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{structures}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{on}\hskip 2.84544ptV\}

such that
\psi_{\rho,X_{\mathbf{P}}}=j_{\rho}\circ\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}
. Furthermore
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}
is
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant by definition of the
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-action on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
.

With this result in hand, one can make sense of the following definition:

Definition 2.26

(
[
39
, Def. 3.4]
)
.

A
(connected) mixed Hodge datum
is a triple
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, where:

(a)

\mathbf{P}
is a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group;

(b)

X_{\mathbf{P}}
is the
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})
of a morphism
x_{0}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying axioms
(i)-(iv)
of Lemma
2.13
;

(c)

D_{\mathbf{P}}
is a connected component of the complex manifold
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
associated in Proposition
2.24
to
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}})
.

A complex manifold of the form
D_{\mathbf{P}}
for a (connected) mixed Hodge datum
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is called a
(connected) Mumford-Tate domain
. It is a
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Remark 2.27
.

In the sequel, we will only work with connected Hodge data and connected Mumford-Tate domains in the terminology of
[
39
]
, and we will omit the adjective "connected" which we still added in the definition in order to be consistent with the litterature.

The following is an immediate consequence of
[
43
, (1.7)]
.

Lemma 2.28
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
be two mixed Hodge data and let
i:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
be a group morphism such that post-composition by
i_{\mathbb{C}}
maps
X_{\mathbf{P}}
in
X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
. Then, the map
X_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
descends to a holomorphic map
\phi:\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
. If furthermore
i
is injective, the map
\phi
is a closed complex-analytic immersion.

Definition 2.29

(
[
39
, Def. 3.5]
)
.

A
morphism of mixed Hodge data

(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
is a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups
i:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
such that post-composition of morphisms by
i_{\mathbb{C}}
induces a map
X_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
, and the induced holomorphic map
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
maps
D_{\mathbf{P}}
into
D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}
. A
mixed Hodge subdatum
of some mixed Hodge datum
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
is a mixed Hodge datum
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
such that
\mathbf{P}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
and the inclusion
\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
is a morphism of mixed Hodge data.

Example 2.30
.

Fix the datum of

•

a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
V
endowed with an increasing finite filtration
W_{\bullet}
by
\mathbb{Q}
-vector subspaces;

•

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
a
(-1)^{k}
-symmetric non-degenerate bilinear form
q_{k}:\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{W}(V)\times\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{W}(V)\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
;

•

a sequence of integers
(h^{p,q})_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
h^{p,q}=h^{q,p}
for every
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\sum_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}h^{p,q}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}V
.

Let
M
(resp.
\check{M}
) be the set of decreasing finite filtrations
F^{\bullet}
of
V_{\mathbb{C}}
such that the triple
(V,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure which is graded-polarized by
(q_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
(resp. such that for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the form
q_{k}
satisfies condition (
2.1.1
) of Definition
2.4
) and with Hodge numbers
(h^{p,q})_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Clearly
\check{M}
is closed in a product of Grassmanian hence inherits the structure of complex algebraic variety. For this structure,
M
is clearly a semi-algebraic open subset of
\check{M}
, hence there is an induced structure of complex manifold on
M
. Let
\mathbf{P}^{M}=\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
. Let
x_{0}
be a bigraduation of some mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
h_{0}\in M
. Let
X^{M}
be the
\mathbf{P}^{M}(\mathbb{R})(\mathbf{P}^{M})^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
x_{0}
in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{P}^{M}_{\mathbb{C}})
. Then for any connected component
M^{+}
of
M
(for the topology induced by the above defined complex-analytic structure on
M
) the triplet
(\mathbf{P}^{M},X^{M},M^{+})
is a mixed Hodge datum. Such a mixed Hodge datum is called a
period mixed Hodge datum

Remark 2.31
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge datum and
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
a faithful representation. Then there exists a datum
((q_{k}),(h^{p,q}))
and a
\mathbf{P}^{M}(\mathbb{R})(\mathbf{P}^{M})^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-conjugacy class
X^{M}
as in the latter example such that
\rho
factors through
\mathbf{P}^{M}
and the induced inclusion
\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{M}
defines a morphism of mixed Hodge data
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}^{M},X^{M},M^{+})
for some connected component
M^{+}
of
M
.

Mixed Hodge classes

Mixed Hodge data have the bad feature of fixing the conjugacy class
X_{\mathbf{P}}
of bigraduations, which might not contain all bigraduations of mixed Hodge structures in
D_{\mathbf{P}}
. In particular, for
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
the conjugacy class
X_{\mathbf{P}}
might not contain the canonical bigraduation of
h
. Furthermore, this constrains (unnecessarily, as one is primarily interested in mixed Hodge structures, not bigraduations) the notion of morphisms of mixed Hodge data. To palliate to this problem we make the following:

Definition 2.32
.

(a)

Two mixed Hodge data
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
are called
essentialy equivalent
if there exists an isomorphism
i:\mathbf{P}\cong\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
such that for any faithful representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
, one has
j_{\rho\circ i}(D_{\mathbf{P}})=j_{\rho}(D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
. This defines an equivalence relation on the set of mixed Hodge data, called the
essential equivalence relation
.

(b)

A
mixed Hodge class
is an equivalence class, denoted
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, of mixed Hodge data for the essential equivalence relation. A mixed Hodge datum in the equivalence class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is called a
representative of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

(c)

A
morphism of mixed Hodge classes

(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
is a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups
\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
which defines a morphism of mixed Hodge data between some representatives
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
of the classes
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
.

(d)

A
mixed Hodge sub-class
of a mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
is a mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
endowed with an inclusion of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups
\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
which is a morphism of mixed Hodge classes.

Remark 2.33
.

Let
i:(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
be a morphism of mixed Hodge classes. For any representative
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, there exists a unique representative
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
of
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
such that
i:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}^{\prime}
defines a morphism of mixed Hodge data
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{P}^{\prime},X_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})
.

Lemma 2.34
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a mixed Hodge sub-class of a mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, and
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
be the inclusion. Let
(\mathbf{M},X_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
, resp.
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, be representatives of
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
, resp.
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, such that
i
makes the former a mixed Hodge sub-datum of the latter. By Lemma
2.28
, these choices give rise to a complex-analytic closed immersion

\phi:D_{\mathbf{M}}\hookrightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}

induced by the composition-by-
i
map
X_{\mathbf{M}}\rightarrow X_{\mathbf{P}}
. Then
\phi
doesn’t depend on the choice of representatives of the classes
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
and
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. It is denoted
\phi_{i}
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathbf{M},X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
and
(\mathbf{P},X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be another pair of representatives such that the inclusion
i
makes the first a mixed Hodge sub-datum of the second. By definition, one has a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X_{\mathbf{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ(-)}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X_{\mathbf{M}}}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}}}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ(-)}
\textstyle{X_{\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{P}}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}}}

We are tasked with proving that for any
h\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
one has
\phi(h)=\phi^{\prime}(h)
. Let
x\in X_{\mathbf{M}}
and
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}
such that
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{M}}}(x)=\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}}(x^{\prime})=h
. By definition, one has
\phi(h)=\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}(i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x)
and
\phi^{\prime}(h)=\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}}(i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x^{\prime})
. Let
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
be a faithful representation. Since

j_{\rho}:D_{\mathbf{P}}\hookrightarrow\{\mathrm{mixed}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbb{Q}\mathrm{-Hodge}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{structures}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{on}\hskip 2.84544ptV\}

is injective, it suffices to check that
j_{\rho}(\phi(h))=j_{\rho}(\phi^{\prime}(h))
. To see this, recall that
j_{\rho}\circ\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}
maps a morphism
x_{\mathbf{P}}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
to the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure defined by
\rho_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x_{\mathbf{P}}
. Therefore,

j_{\rho}(\phi(h))=j_{\rho}(\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}(i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x))=j_{\rho\circ i}(\psi_{X_{\mathbf{M}}}(x)),

and similarly,

j_{\rho}(\phi^{\prime}(h))=j_{\rho}(\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}}(i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x^{\prime}))=j_{\rho\circ i}(\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}}(x^{\prime})).

Since by assumption
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{M}}}(x)=\psi_{X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}}(x^{\prime})
, this implies that
\phi(h)=\phi^{\prime}(h)
as desired.
∎

Convention 2.35
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
a mixed Hodge subclass, with underlying group inclusion
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
. In the sequel, we will identify
D_{\mathbf{M}}
with the complex submanifold
\phi_{i}(D_{\mathbf{M}})
of
D_{\mathbf{P}}
. It is an
\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{M}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
D_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Remark 2.36
.

It makes sense to talk about
\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{M}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
D_{\mathbf{P}}
as above, because in the setting of the above convention, Lemma
2.15
ensures that
\mathbf{M}^{u}(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
.

Mixed Hodge structures with bounded Mumford-Tate group

We can now turn to the proof of the following which states that Mumford-Tate subdomains indeed parametrize mixed Hodge structures with bounded Mumford-Tate group:

Proposition 2.37
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
the inclusion of a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup. Let
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
. The following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

for any faithful
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
of
\mathbf{P}
, the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
j_{\rho}(h)
has Mumford-Tate group contained in
\mathbf{M}
;

(ii)

for some faithful
\mathbb{Q}
-representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
of
\mathbf{P}
, the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
j_{\rho}(h)
has Mumford-Tate group contained in
\mathbf{M}
;

(iii)

there exists a mixed Hodge sub-class
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
with underlying inclusion
i
, such that
h\in D_{\mathbf{M}}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Proof.

(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
: Immediate.

(ii)\Rightarrow(iii)
: Assume
(ii)
, let
\rho:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
be as in the statement and denote by
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
the inclusion. By abuse of notation, we shall identify
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{M}
to connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
using
\rho
and
\rho\circ i
. Let
W_{\bullet}
be the induced weight filtration on
V
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be some representative of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
x_{0}\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
some lift of
h
. By definition of mixed Hodge data
\rho_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x_{0}
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
, hence by the fourth assertion of Lemma
2.13
, there is some graded-polarization
(q_{k})
of
j_{\rho}(h)
such that
\mathbf{P}\subset\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
.

Let
x_{\mathrm{Del}}
be the canonical bigraduation of the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
j_{\rho}(h)
. By assumption
\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))\subset\mathbf{M}
, and by Proposition
2.21
(ii) one has the inclusion
x_{\mathrm{Del}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))_{\mathbb{C}}
, hence
