x_{\mathrm{Del}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{C}}
. Since
\mathbf{M}
is contained in
\mathbf{P}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
, and
\mathbf{P}
is contained in
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
as just proven, it follows that
\mathbf{M}
is too. Lemma
2.15
then implies that
x_{\mathrm{Del}}
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{M}
. Let
X_{\mathbf{M}}
be the
\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{M}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
x_{\mathrm{Del}}
in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{C}})
, let
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{M}}
be the image of

\psi_{\rho\circ i,X_{\mathbf{M}}}:X_{\mathbf{M}}\rightarrow\{\mathrm{mixed}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbb{Q}\mathrm{-Hodge}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{structures}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{on}\hskip 2.84544ptV\}.

Finally, let
D_{\mathbf{M}}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{M}}
containing
j_{\rho}(x)
. By construction
(\mathbf{M},X_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a mixed Hodge datum. Denote by
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
the associated mixed Hodge class.

We now proceed to prove that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a mixed Hodge sub-class of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. We saw that
\mathbf{P}\subset\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
and that
x_{\mathrm{Del}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
, hence Lemma
2.15
ensures that
x_{\mathrm{Del}}
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
. Using this to identify
x_{\mathrm{Del}}
to a
\mathbb{C}
-morphism
\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
, let
X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}
be the
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-conjugacy class of
x_{\mathrm{Del}}
in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})
and
\mathcal{D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}}
be the image of

\psi_{\rho,X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}}:X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\{\mathrm{mixed}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbb{Q}\mathrm{-Hodge}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{structures}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{on}\hskip 2.84544ptV\},

and
D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}
containing
j_{\rho}(x)
. The
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbits
D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}}
and
j_{\rho}(D_{\mathbf{P}})
in the set of mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures on
V
both contain
j_{\rho}(h)
hence are equal, and this is easily seen to be true for any faithful representation of
\mathbf{P}
. Hence
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{P},X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
are essentially equivalent, and by construction, the inclusion
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
makes
(\mathbf{M},X_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
a mixed Hodge subdatum of
(\mathbf{P},X^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Hence
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, and by construction,
j_{\rho}(h)\in D_{\mathbf{M}}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}
. This proves
(ii)\Rightarrow(iii)
.

(iii)\Rightarrow(i)
: Assume
(iii)
, and let
\rho:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
be any faithful
\mathbb{Q}
-representation of
\mathbf{P}
. Let
(\mathbf{M},X_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a representative of the mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
whose existence is guaranteed by
(iii)
. Let
x\in X_{\mathbf{M}}
such that
\psi_{\rho\circ i,X_{\mathbf{M}}}(x)=j_{\rho}(h)
. Then the
\mathbb{C}
-morphism
\rho_{\mathbb{C}}\circ i_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)_{\mathbb{C}}
is a bigraduation of
j_{\rho}(h)
such that
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{C}}
. With the notations of Proposition
2.21
, this implies
\mathbf{P}(x)\subset\mathbf{M}
. But by Proposition
2.21
, one has
\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))\subset\mathbf{P}(x)
. Therefore, the Mumford-Tate group
\psi_{\rho}(x)
is indeed contained in
\mathbf{M}
. This proves
(iii)\Rightarrow(i)
and completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.38
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
. For any pair of faithful representations
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
and
\rho^{\prime}:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V^{\prime})
, the Mumford-Tate groups of the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures
j_{\rho}(h)
and
j_{\rho^{\prime}}(h)
are connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroups of
\mathbf{P}
and are equal as subgroups of
\mathbf{P}
.

Proof.

Let
\rho
,
\rho^{\prime}
and
h
as in the statement. Let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a representative of the mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Let
x\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
be such that
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}(x)=h
. Then by Proposition
2.21
(i), one finds that
\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))\subset\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho^{\prime}}(h))\subset\mathbf{P}
. Applying equivalence between
(i)
and
(ii)
in Proposition
2.37
with
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))
gives that
\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho^{\prime}}(h))\subset\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))
. Applying the same equivalence with
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho^{\prime}}(h))
gives that
\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho}(h))\subset\mathbf{P}(j_{\rho^{\prime}}(h))
, as desired.
∎

Definition 2.39
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class, and
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
. The
Mumford-Tate group of
h
, denoted
\mathbf{P}(h)
, is the Mumford-Tate group of the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
j_{\rho}(h)
for some faithful representation
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
. It is a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}
, as such independent of
\rho
.

We conclude this subsection with the following two consequences of Proposition
2.37

Corollary 2.40
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class, let
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
the inclusion of a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}
,and
X\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}
a connected subset such that for any
h\in X
, one has
\mathbf{P}(h)\subset\mathbf{M}
. Then, there exists a unique mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
with underlying inclusion
i
such that
X\subset D_{\mathbf{M}}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Proof.

By the previous two results, we only have to prove that for any two mixed Hodge subclasses
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),(\mathbf{M},D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
with the same underlying inclusion of
\mathbb{Q}
-groups
i:\mathbf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
, the two (connected) subsets
D_{\mathbf{M}}
and
D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}
of
D_{\mathbf{P}}
are either equal or disjoint. Assume that they are not disjoint, and
h
be a point in the intersection. Then
D_{\mathbf{M}}
and
D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}
are both
\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{M}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbits in
D_{\mathbf{P}}
containing
h
. Hence they are equal.
∎

Torsion mixed Hodge subclasses

To finish this section, we show that every mixed Hodge class contains a non-trivial mixed Hodge subclass, a fact analogous to the existence of CM points in every Shimura variety for example (and whose proof is similar).

Definition 2.41
.

A mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is called
torsion
if the group
\mathbf{M}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-torus.

Note that if
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a torsion mixed Hodge class, then
\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}=0
.

Proposition 2.42
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class with
\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}>0
. There exists a strict torsion mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{T},D_{\mathbf{T}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a representative of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, let
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
and
x\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
such that
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}(x)=h
. Let
G_{\mathbb{C}}
be a Levi factor of
\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
containing
x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})
. Let
\mathbf{L}
be a Levi factor (defined over
\mathbb{Q}
) of the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathbf{P}
. Then
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{C}}
is a Levi factor of
\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}
hence
[
15
, Prop. 5.1]
ensures that there exists a
u\in\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
such that
uG_{\mathbb{C}}u^{-1}=\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{C}}
. Letting
\pi:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u}
be the quotient and
i:\mathbf{L}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
be the inclusion, we get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{int}_{u}\circ x}
\textstyle{\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathbb{C}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathbb{C}}}
\textstyle{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u})_{\mathbb{C}}}

where
(\pi\circ i)_{\mathbb{C}}
is an isomorphism by definition of Levi factors, and the morphism
x_{u}
exists because
\mathrm{int}_{u}\circ x(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset uG_{\mathbb{C}}u^{-1}=\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{C}}
. Since
\mathrm{int}_{u}\circ x
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}
, we know that
\pi_{\mathbb{C}}\circ\mathrm{int}_{u}\circ x
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
. As the isomorphism
(\pi\circ i)_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, hence over
\mathbb{R}
, it follows that
x_{u}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
, and we see it from now as a morphism of real algebraic groups
x_{u}:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
Z
be a maximal
\mathbb{R}
-torus in
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{R}}
containing
x_{u}(\mathbb{S})
. Let
\mathbf{T}
be a maximal
\mathbb{Q}
-torus in
\mathbf{L}
. Then
\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{R}}
is a maximal
\mathbb{R}
-torus in
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbb{R}}
hence there exists a
g\in\mathbf{L}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
such that
gZg^{-1}=\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
y=\mathrm{int}_{g}\circ\mathrm{int}_{u}\circ x
. By construction we find that
y\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
, that
h^{\prime}:=\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}(y)\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
and
y(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset\mathbf{T}_{\mathbb{C}}
. It then follows from Proposition
2.21
(i)
that
\mathbf{P}(h^{\prime})\subset\mathbf{T}
. Finally Corollary
2.40
applied to
X=\{h^{\prime}\}
ensure the existence of a mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{T},D_{\mathbf{T}})
containing
h^{\prime}
. It is torsion because
\mathbf{T}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-torus by definition, and it is strict because
\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}>0
.
∎

Infinitesimal structure of Mumford-Tate domains

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
. By construction
D_{\mathbf{P}}
is a dense real-analytic open subset of its
compact dual

\check{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
which is
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit. Let
\mu_{h}:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\check{D}_{\mathbf{P}},g\mapsto g\cdot h
be the orbit map. The stabilizer of
h
in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{C})
is
\exp(F_{h}^{0}\mathfrak{p})
where
\mathfrak{p}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
and
F_{h}^{\bullet}\mathfrak{p}
is the Hodge filtration on
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}
defined by
h
. Therefore, one has an isomorphism

\Theta^{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})}_{h}:T_{h}D_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}/F_{h}^{0}\mathfrak{p}

satisfying the following functoriality properties:

(i)

the derivative of the orbit map
d_{1}\mu_{h}:\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow T_{h}D_{\mathbf{P}}
composed with
\Theta^{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})}_{h}
coincides with the quotient map by
F^{0}_{h}\mathfrak{p}
;

(ii)

If
(R,f):(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is a morphism of mixed Hodge classes, and
h\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
then

d_{h}f=\Theta^{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})}_{f(h)}\circ r_{0}\circ(\Theta^{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})}_{h})^{-1}

where
r_{0}:\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbb{C}}/F^{0}_{h}\mathfrak{m}\rightarrow\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}/F^{0}_{f(h)}\mathfrak{p}
is the map induced by the morphism of mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures
dR:\mathfrak{m}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})\rightarrow\mathfrak{p}
.

The Griffiths’ transversality axiom for variations of mixed Hodge structures gives a special role to the subspace

T_{h}^{\mathrm{horiz}}D_{\mathbf{P}}:=(\Theta^{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})}_{h})^{-1}\big(F_{h}^{-1}\mathfrak{p}/F_{h}^{0}\mathfrak{p}\big)\subset T_{h}D_{\mathbf{P}}

which we will refer to as the
horizontal tangent space
in the sequel.

It will sometimes be convenient to work with refined identifications induced by bigraduations. Let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a representative of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
x\in\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}^{-1}(h)
. Let

\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p+q\leqslant 0}\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{p,q}

be the corresponding bigraduation of
\mathfrak{p}
. Equation (
2.1.4
) gives an isomorphism

\Theta^{(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})}_{x}:T_{h}D_{\mathbf{P}}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+q\leqslant 0\\
p<0\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{p,q}

such that

\Theta^{(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})}_{x}\big(T_{h}^{\mathrm{horiz}}D_{\mathbf{P}}\big)=\bigoplus_{q\leqslant 1}\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{-1,q}.

Mixed Hodge varieties

In the study of variations of mixed Hodge structures, the dependance of parallel transport (to identify two fibers of the variation) on a choice of path joining the base points forces to work with mixed Hodge structures up to integral equivalence. We recall in this section the construction, again due to Klingler, of appropriate parameter spaces that allow to deal with such considerations.

Consider a mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, and let
\Gamma\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}:=\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
be an neat arithmetic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
(see
[
16
, (17.1)]
). As a subgroup of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, the group
\Gamma
acts on the left on the
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-homogeneous space
D_{\mathbf{P}}
. Since
\Gamma
is torsion-free, it acts freely on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
and since the complex structure on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
is
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-invariant, the quotient
\Gamma\backslash D_{\mathbf{P}}
has a natural structure of complex manifold, and the projection
\pi_{\Gamma}:D_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\Gamma\backslash D_{\mathbf{P}}
is holomorphic (and is even a local biholomorphism on the source).

Definition 2.43

(
[
39
, Def. 3.8]
)
.

A
mixed Hodge variety
is a smooth complex-analytic variety of the form
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}=\Gamma\backslash D_{\mathbf{P}}
for a mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and a neat arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
.

Let
i:(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subset(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge subclass. Denote by
\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}
the neat arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma\cap\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
of
\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. Since the map
\phi_{i}
is equivariant for the inclusion
\mathbf{M}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, it descends to a holomorphic map

\phi_{i,\Gamma}:\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}.

Lemma 2.44
.

The image
\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}})
is a smooth locally closed complex-analytic subset of the mixed Hodge variety
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
.

Proof.

By definition, the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\Gamma}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{i,\Gamma}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}}

hence
\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}})=\pi_{\Gamma}(\phi_{i}(D_{\mathbf{M}}))
. But
\phi_{i}
is a complex-analytic closed immersion, hence
\phi_{i}(D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a smooth closed complex-analytic subset of
D_{\mathbf{P}}
, and the result follows from the fact that
\pi_{\Gamma}
is a local biholomorphism on the source.
∎

Definition 2.45
.

Let
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
be a mixed Hodge variety, and
i:(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subset(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
a mixed Hodge subclass. The
special subvariety of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
associated to
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is the smooth locally closed complex-analytic subset
\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}})
of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
.

We will also need the following notion in the proof of Theorem
9.1
:

Definition 2.46
.

Let
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
be a mixed Hodge variety. A
torsion point
in
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
is a special subvariety associated to a torsion mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

Variations of mixed Hodge structures and their period maps

Most of the material of this section is classical, see for example
[
41
,
39
,
32
]
.

Definitions

Variations of mixed Hodge structures

Let
S
be a connected complex manifold. Following Steenbrink-Zucker in
[
46
]
, an
integral graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures over
S
is the datum of

•

a local system of torsion-free finitely generated abelian groups
\mathbb{V}
over
S
;

•

an increasing exhausting finite filtration
\mathbb{W}_{\bullet}
, the
weight filtration
, of
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{Q}}
by
\mathbb{Q}
-local subsystems;

•

a descending exhausting finite filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}
, the
Hodge filtration
, of
\mathcal{V}:=\mathbb{V}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathcal{O}_{S}
by locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-submodules;

•

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
a flat
(-1)^{k}
-symmetric bilinear form
\underline{q_{k}}:\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{\mathbb{W}}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{\mathbb{W}}\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb{Q}}
;

subject to the following axioms:

•

denoting
\nabla=id\otimes\mathrm{d}
the integrable connection on
\mathcal{V}
induced by the flat structure on
\mathbb{V}
, Griffiths transversality is satisfied:

\nabla(\mathcal{F}^{p})\subset\mathcal{F}^{p-1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\Omega^{1}_{S};

•

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and every
s\in S(\mathbb{C})
, the filtration induced by
\mathcal{F}_{s}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{\mathbb{W}_{s}}(\mathbb{V}_{s})_{\mathbb{Q}}
defines a pure Hodge structure of weight
k
on the latter, polarized by
(\underline{q_{k}})_{s}
.

Remark 3.1
.

In the particular case where
\mathbb{W}
consists of exactly one non-trivial step, one recovers the usual notion of integral polarized variation of (pure) Hodge structures, except that the polarization is only defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, and not over
\mathbb{Z}
as usual. Of course it doesn’t make any substantial difference, as this issue can be solved by scaling the polarization form by a positive integer, which preserves the axioms of polarizations.

Admissibility

In order to get a theory of the asymptotic behaviour of
\mathbb{V}
at infinity which is similar to the pure case, one has to add a condition on
\mathbb{V}
which we recall now, introduced by Steenbrink-Zucker in
[
46
]
when
S
is a curve and generalized by Kashiwara in
[
35
]
for higher dimensional
S
.

To recall it, we start with some linear algebra from
[
46
, §2]
. Let
V
be a finite dimensional vector space over a characteristic
0
field and
N:V\rightarrow V
a nilpotent endomorphism of
V
. As shown for example in
[
45
, Lem. 6.4]
, there exists a unique increasing finite filtration
M_{\bullet}
of
V
such that for every
l\geqslant 0
,
N(M_{l})\subseteq M_{l-2}
and the induced map
N^{l}:\mathrm{Gr}^{M}_{l}V\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{M}_{-l}V
is an isomorphism. It is called
the weight filtration of
V
for
N
.

Assume furthermore,
V
is endowed with an increasing finite filtration by sub-vector spaces
W_{\bullet}
such that for every
k\lx@nobreakspace\in\mathbb{Z}
one has
N(W_{k})\subset W_{k}
so that
N
induces an endomorphism
{{}^{k}}N
of
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{k}V
. Denote by
{{}^{k}}M_{\bullet}
the weight filtration of
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{k}V
for
{{}^{k}}N
. Then following
[
46
, Def. 2.5]
, a
weight filtration of
V
for
N
relative to
W_{\bullet}
is an increasing finite filtration
M_{\bullet}
of
V
such that

•

N(M_{l})\subset M_{l-2}
for every
l\in\mathbb{Z}
;

•

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the filtration induced by
M_{\bullet}
on
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{k}V
coincides with
{{}^{k}}M
.

By
[
46
, Prop. 2.6]
, this filtration is unique if it exists.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an integral graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures over the punctured disk
\Delta^{\times}
. Let
s\in\Delta^{\times}
and
T\in\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s,\mathbb{Q}})
the image of a generator of
\pi_{1}(\Delta^{\times})
under the monodromy representation at
s
associated to
\mathbb{V}
. If
T
is unipotent, Deligne proved in
[
27
]
that the vector bundle with connection
(\mathcal{V}:=\mathbb{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta^{\times}},\nabla=\mathrm{id}\otimes d)
extends uniquely as a vector bundle with connection with at worse logarithmic poles at
0
denoted
(\overline{\mathcal{V}},\bar{\nabla})
, over
\Delta
such that the residue of the connection at
0
is nilpotent. The residue of the connection at
0
is then the nilpotent logarithm
N:=\log(T)
, defined by the usual power series which converges because
T
is unipotent. Furthermore, this construction defines an exact functor, so that for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
one has a canonical extension
\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{k}
of
\mathcal{W}_{k}:=\mathbb{W}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta^{\times}}
which is naturally a sub-vector bundle of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}
. Finally, remark that since the weight filtration is by local sub-systems, the
(\mathbb{W}_{k})_{s}
are preserved by the action of
T
hence by the action of
N
. So it makes sense to talk about the weight filtration of
\mathbb{V}_{s}
for
N
relative to
(\mathbb{W}_{\bullet})_{s}

We can now turn to the promised definition. With the notations above, the variation
\mathbb{V}
is called
admissible
if the following axioms hold:

(A_{0})

T
is unipotent;

(A_{1})

There exists a weight filtration of
\mathbb{V}_{s}
for
N
relative to
(\mathbb{W}_{\bullet})_{s}
;

(A_{2})

The Hodge filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}
extends to a filtration
\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
of
\overline{\mathcal{V}}
by locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}
-modules such that for each
k,p\in\mathbb{Z}
the
\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}
-module
\mathrm{Gr}_{\overline{\mathcal{F}}}^{p}\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{\overline{\mathcal{W}}}\overline{\mathcal{V}}
is locally free.

Now let
S
be a smooth irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety and choose a log-smooth compactification
(\overline{S},E)
of
S
. Let
\mathbb{V}
be an integral graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures on
S
. It is called
admissible with respect to
(\overline{S},E)
if for every holomorphic map
i:\Delta\rightarrow\overline{S}
which maps
\Delta^{\times}
in
S
such that the pulled-back variation
i^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
has unipotent monodromy, the varation
i^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
over
\Delta^{\times}
is admissible in the above sense. The following ensures that this property doesn’t depend on the initial choice of a compactification:

Lemma 3.2

(
[
5
, Prop. 4.2]
)
.

Let
(\overline{S}^{\prime},E^{\prime})
be an other log-smooth compactification. Then
\mathbb{V}
is admissible with respect to
(\overline{S}^{\prime},E^{\prime})
if and only if it is admissible with respect to
(\overline{S},E)
.

We are lead to the:

Definition 3.3

(
[
41
, Def. 14.49]
)
.

The variation
\mathbb{V}
is called
admissible
if it is admissible with respect to some (hence any) log-smooth compactification of
S
.

Generic Mumford-Tate group and monodromy

Let
S
be a smooth, irreducible and quasi-projective complex algebraic variety, and
\mathbb{V}
a graded-polarized and integral variation of mixed Hodge structures over
S
. Let
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Generic Hodge tensors

Definition 3.4
.

A
generic Hodge tensor at
s
for
\mathbb{V}
is a tensor
\zeta\in T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
such that for any (possibly constant) path
\tau:s\rightarrow s^{\prime}
in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
, the parallel transport of
\zeta
along
\tau
to
T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}})
is a Hodge tensor for the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
T^{a,b}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}})
induced from that on
\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}}
.

We will denote by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{s}
the graded vector space of generic Hodge tensors at
s
for
\mathbb{V}
. There is a chain of inclusions of graded vector spaces

\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{s}\subset\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}_{s}^{\otimes})\subset T^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}_{s})

We will also sometimes refer to tensors in
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}_{s}^{\otimes})-\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{s}
as
exceptional Hodge tensors at
s
. From the definition,
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{s}
is preserved by the action of
\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s)
on
T^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
. A consequence of the results recalled in this paragraph is that this action factors through a finite quotient of
\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s)
.

Definition 3.5
.

The
generic Mumford-Tate group of
(\mathbb{V},S)
at
s
is the subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{s}
of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
fixing all generic Hodge tensors at
s
for
\mathbb{V}
.

The following is immediate from the definition.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
. Then
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{s}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
.

Before proceeding, we record the following two easy results for later use.

Lemma 3.7

(
[
1
, Lem. 4]
)
.

There exists a countable union
S^{\circ}
of proper closed analytic subvarieties of
S^{\mathrm{an}}
such that for any
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}-S^{\circ}
, the inclusion
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subset\mathbf{P}_{s}
is an equality.

Definition 3.8
.

A point
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
such that the inclusion
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subset\mathbf{P}_{s}
is an equality is called
Hodge generic

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
and
\tau:[0,1]\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{an}}
be a continuous path joining
s
to
s^{\prime}
. Let
\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}}:\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}})
be the induced parallel transport isomorphism. Then
\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}}(\mathbf{P}_{s})=\mathbf{P}_{s^{\prime}}
. In particular,
\dim\mathbf{P}_{s}=\dim\mathbf{P}_{s^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau_{\ast}:\mathbb{V}_{s}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}}
be the parallel transport operator at the level of
\mathbb{V}
. By definition, for
g\in\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
, one has
\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}}(g)=\tau_{\ast}\circ g\circ\tau_{\ast}^{-1}
. Hence the image by
\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}}
of the fixator of tensors
(t_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
is the fixator of the tensors
(\tau_{\ast}(t_{\alpha}))_{\alpha}
. Since by definition,
\tau_{\ast}
maps generic Hodge tensors at
s
bijectively to generic Hodge tensors at
s^{\prime}
, the result follows.
∎

Algebraic monodromy group

Let
\rho_{s}:\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s)\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
the monodromy representation, at the base point
s
, associated to the local system underlying
\mathbb{V}
.

Definition 3.10
.

The algebraic monodromy group of
\mathbb{V}
at
s
is the identity connected component
\mathbf{H}_{s}
of the
\mathbb{Q}
-Zariski closure of
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{s})
.

Assume until the end of the subsection that
\mathbb{V}
is admissible. The following result of André, due to Deligne in the pure case, is a cornerstone of the theory and will be of constant use in the sequel:

Theorem 3.11

(
[
1
]
)
.

The algebraic monodromy group
\mathbf{H}_{s}
of
\mathbb{V}
at
s
is a normal subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
, and is contained in the derived subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{s}^{\mathrm{der}}
of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
. In particular, the radical of
\mathbf{H}_{s}
is unipotent.

Proof.

The proof of
[
1
, Thm. 1]
shows that
\mathbf{H}_{s}
is a subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{s}^{\mathrm{der}}
and is normal in
\mathbf{P}_{s}
. The second statement is
[
1
, Cor. 2]
.
∎

Let us record the following fact which is an easy consequence of the previous result, and will be useful for us.

Corollary 3.12
.

There exists a finite étale cover
\pi:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
such that after replacing
S
by
S^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{V}
by
\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
, for any
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
, the image of
\rho_{s}
is contained in a neat arithmetic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

Let
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
and let
\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{s}
be the
\mathbb{Q}
-Zariski closure of
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{s})
in
\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})
. It is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})
whose
\mathbb{Q}
-points contain
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{s})
. Its identity connected component is by definition
\mathbf{H}_{s}
. Therefore
\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})/\mathbf{H}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
is finite. The kernel of the composition of
\rho_{s}
with the projection
\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})/\mathbf{H}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
is a finite index normal subgroup of
\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s)
. Denote by
\pi:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
the corresponding finite étale cover of
S
, whose construction is easily seen by direct computation not to depend on the choice of base point
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
. In the sequel of the proof, we replace
S
by
S^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{V}
by
\pi^{\ast}(\mathbb{V})
. By construction of
\pi
, for any
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
one has that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{s})\subset\mathbf{H}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})\subset\mathbf{P}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Since the local system
\mathbb{V}
is integral one has on the other hand that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{s})\subset\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{Z},s})
. This implies that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{s})
is contained in the arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma_{0}=\mathrm{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{Z},s})\cap\mathbf{P}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
of
\mathbf{P}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
. By
[
14
, Prop. 17.4]
there exists a neat finite index subgroup
\Gamma
of
\Gamma_{0}
, which is therefore a neat arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
of
\mathbf{P}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})
. After passing to the finite etale cover of
S
corresponding to the kernel of
\rho_{s}:\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s)\rightarrow\Gamma_{0}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0}/\Gamma
, the image of
\rho_{s}
becomes contained in
\Gamma
.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

Let
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
, let
W_{\bullet}
be the weight filtration on
\mathbb{V}_{s}
and
(q_{k})
the graded-polarization at
s
. As by definition
\mathbb{W}_{\bullet}
and
(\underline{q}_{k})
are flat, the image of
\rho_{s}
lies in
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}(\mathbb{Q})
(without having to pass to a finite étale cover).

Generic mixed Hodge class and the normalized period map

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures over a smooth, irreducible and quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Fix a base point
s_{0}\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
, let
\pi:\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{an}}
be the universal cover of
S^{\mathrm{an}}
and fix a lift
\tilde{s}_{0}
of
s_{0}
in
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
. Denote by
\rho=\rho_{s_{0}}
be the monodromy representation at
s_{0}
, by
W_{\bullet}=(\mathbb{W}_{\bullet})_{s_{0}}
the weight filtration on
\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}}
, by
q_{k}=(\underline{q_{k}})_{s_{0}}
(
k\in\mathbb{Z}
) the polarisations on the graded pieces and by
(h^{p,q})_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}
the Hodge numbers of
(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}},W_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}_{s_{0}})
.

Construction of the mixed Hodge data

Let
M_{s_{0}}
be the parameter space for mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures on
\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}}
with weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
, Hodge numbers
(h^{p,q})
and graded-polarized by the
q_{k}
’s. Let
m_{0}
be the point of
M_{s_{0}}
corresponding to the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}},W_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}_{s_{0}})
, and let
x_{0}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}})_{\mathbb{C}}
be the canonical bigraduation of
m_{0}
.

Lemma 3.14
.

The morphism
x_{0}
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
and
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
.

Proof.

By flatness of the weight filtration and the polarization, one has
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}\subset\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}:=\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet},(q_{k})}
. Furthermore, as
\mathbf{P}(m_{0})=\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}})\subset\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}})
, one has by Proposition
2.37
that
x_{0}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}})\subset(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
. Hence by Lemma
2.15
the bigraduation
x_{0}
satisfies Pink’s axioms for
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
and
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
, as claimed.
∎

Let
X^{M_{s_{0}}}
(resp.
X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
) be the
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit (resp.
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})
) of
x_{0}
in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}_{\mathbb{C}})
(resp. in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}})_{\mathbb{C}})
). Let
{M_{s_{0}}}^{+}
be the connected component of
{M_{s_{0}}}
containing
m_{0}
, and
D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
containing
m_{0}
. By construction, the inclusion
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
makes
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
a mixed Hodge sub-datum of
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},X^{M_{s_{0}}},{M_{s_{0}}}^{+})
. A priori, they both depend on the choice of
s_{0}
.

The lifted period map

Since
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
is simply-connected, the local system
\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
on
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
is trivial and inherits a graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures. By triviality of the local system
\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
, there is for every
\tilde{s}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
a canonical isomorphism
i_{\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{0}}:\mathbb{V}_{\pi(\tilde{s})}=(\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V})_{\tilde{s}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}}
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}:\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow M_{s_{0}}
be the map which associates to
\tilde{s}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure
(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}},W_{\bullet},i_{\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{0}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}))
image of the mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
(\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V})_{\tilde{s}}
under the isomorphism
i_{\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{0}}
. It has Hodge numbers
(h^{p,q})
, has weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
and is graded-polarized by the
q_{k}
’s by flatness of
\mathbb{W}_{\bullet}
and
\underline{q_{k}}
. Hence
(\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}},W_{\bullet},i_{\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{0}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}))
indeed defines a point of
M_{s_{0}}
. The following is immediate from the definitions:

Lemma 3.15
.

The map
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s_{0}}}
is holomorphic and
\rho
-equivariant, where
\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s_{0})
acts on
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
by deck transformations, and
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on the
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}}(\mathbb{C})
-homogeneous space
M_{s_{0}}
as a subset of
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}}(\mathbb{C})
.

Remark 3.16
.

The
\rho
-equivariance of
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s_{0}}}
ensures that for any arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma^{M_{s_{0}}}
of
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}(\mathbb{R})
containing
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho)
, the map
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}
descends to an analytic map between orbifolds

\Phi_{s_{0}}:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\Gamma^{M_{s_{0}}}\backslash M_{s_{0}}

which is classically referred to as the period map of
\mathbb{V}
. However this construction does not exploit the fact that
\mathbb{V}
might carry non-trivial generic Hodge tensors. The sequel of this subsection is dedicated to explain how one takes this information into account.

As constructed, the map
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}
depends on the choice of
\tilde{s}_{0}
. However the maps resulting from two choices of points of
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
can be canonically identified. More precisely the construction immediately implies the following:

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
\tilde{s}_{1}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
be another base point, and
s_{1}=\pi(\tilde{s}_{1})
. Let
f_{\tilde{s}_{0},\tilde{s}_{1}}:(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}},\tilde{s}_{0})\rightarrow(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}},\tilde{s}_{1})
be the deck transformation of
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
mapping
\tilde{s}_{0}
to
\tilde{s}_{1}
. Let
g_{\tilde{s}_{0},\tilde{s}_{1}}:M_{s_{0}}\rightarrow M_{s_{1}}
the biholomorphism induced by the isomorphism
i_{\tilde{s}_{0},\tilde{s}_{1}}:\mathbb{V}_{s_{0}}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}_{s_{1}}
. Then the diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\tilde{s}_{0},\tilde{s}_{1}}}
\textstyle{M_{s_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\tilde{s}_{0},\tilde{s}_{1}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{1}}}
\textstyle{M_{s_{1}}}

commutes.

The normalized lifted period map

Let
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
(resp.
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},M_{s_{0}}^{+})
) be the essential equivalence class of
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
(resp.
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},X^{M_{s_{0}}},M_{s_{0}}^{+})
).

Lemma 3.18
.

One has
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s_{0}}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})\subset D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
. Furthermore:

(i)

(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
is the unique mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},M_{s_{0}}^{+})
with underlying inclusion
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
whose associated Mumford-Tate domain contains
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})
;

(ii)

(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
is minimal for the inclusion among mixed Hodge subclasses of
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},M_{s_{0}}^{+})
whose associated Mumford-Tate domain contains
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})
.

Proof.

(i)
By definition of the generic Mumford-Tate group
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
of
\mathbb{V}
at
s_{0}
and Lemma
3.9
, every
h\in\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})
satisfies
\mathbf{P}(h)\subset\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
. By Corollary
2.40
, there exists a unique mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
of
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},M_{s_{0}}^{+})
with underlying inclusion
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
such that
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})\subset D^{\prime}_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
. As
m_{0}\in\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})\cap D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
, one has
m_{0}\in D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}^{\prime}\cap D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}\neq\emptyset
and the proof of Corollary
2.40
shows that this mixed Hodge subclass is precisely the essential equivalence class of the mixed Hodge sub-datum
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
of
(\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}},X^{M_{s_{0}}},M_{s_{0}}^{+})
, and we denote it by
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
.

(ii)
Assume that there is a mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subseteq(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
such that
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})\subset D_{\mathbf{M}}
. By Lemma
3.7
, there exists
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})=\mathbf{P}_{s}
. Therefore, for any lift
\tilde{s}
of
s
to
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
, one has
\mathbf{P}(\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\tilde{s}))=\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
. On the other hand, by assumption
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\tilde{s})\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
, so that by Lemma
2.37
one has
\mathbf{P}(\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\tilde{s}))\subset\mathbf{M}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
. This proves
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}\subset\mathbf{M}
hence
\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}=\mathbf{M}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{P}^{M_{s_{0}}}
. By the uniqueness assertion
(i)
, this proves the desired minimality of
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
.
∎

In particular,
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}
factors uniquely through a holomorphic map
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
, which we still denote by
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}
.

Corollary 3.19
.

The essential equivalence class of
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
does not depend on the choice of
s_{0}\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Proof.

Let
s_{1}\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
and
\tilde{s}_{1}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
be a lift of
s_{1}
. Unwrapping definitions, it suffices to prove that
g_{\tilde{s}_{0},\tilde{s}_{1}}(D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})\subset D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{1}}}
, where we use the notation introduced in Lemma
3.17
. This follows from the uniqueness statement in Lemma
3.18
and the commutativity of the diagram in Lemma
3.17
.
∎

Denote by
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
the corresponding mixed Hodge class. Summing up, we have shown that for any
\tilde{s}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
, the map
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}}
factors as a holomorphic map
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}_{\pi(\tilde{s})}}
and that the map obtained for any other choice of base point
\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
can be canonically identified as in Lemma
3.17
.

Definition 3.20
.

(a)

The mixed Hodge datum
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
is called the
generic mixed Hodge datum of
\mathbb{V}
at
s_{0}
;

(b)

The mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is called the
generic mixed Hodge class of
\mathbb{V}
;

(c)

The map
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}:\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
is called the
(normalized) lifted period map of
\mathbb{V}
at
\tilde{s}_{0}
.

As a consequence of Lemma
3.15
and Griffiths’ transversality axiom, one finds:

Lemma 3.21
.

The normalized lifted period map of
\mathbb{V}
at
\tilde{s}_{0}
is holomorphic and horizontal, i.e. for any
\tilde{s}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
the derivative
d_{\tilde{s}}\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}
of
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}
at
\tilde{s}
has image in
T_{\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\tilde{s})}^{\mathrm{horiz}}D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
.

The normalized period map

We saw above that
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})\subset D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
, which implies that

\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt\Phi^{M}\subset\phi_{i,\Gamma^{M}}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma^{M}\cap\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}}).

In general
\Phi^{M}
does not necessarily factor through a map
S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma^{M}\cap\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}}
, as it might happen that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho)
is not contained in full in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. Assume that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho)
is contained in a neat arithmetic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
which, by Corollary
3.12
, can be arranged after replacing
S
by a finite étale cover and
\mathbb{V}
by its pullback along this cover. Then
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}_{0}}:\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
is equivariant under
\rho:\pi_{1}(S^{\mathrm{an}},s_{0})\rightarrow\Gamma\subset\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
and descends to a holomorphic map
\Phi:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
, which does not depend on the initial choice of
\tilde{s}_{0}
as a consequence of Lemma
3.17
and the fact that
\Gamma
contains all parallel transport operators.

Definition 3.22
.

Assume that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho)
is contained in a neat arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. The holomorphic map between complex manifolds
\Phi:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
constructed above is called
the (normalized) period map of
\mathbb{V}
.

Remarkably, the operation that associates to
\mathbb{V}
its normalized period map essentially loses no information, as the following classical result asserts:

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class, let
\Gamma\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
be a neat arithmetic subgroup and
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
. The map which associates to an integral and graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures over
S
with generic mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
the normalized period map of
\mathbb{V}
and the faithful representation
\mathbf{P}\cong\mathbf{P}_{s}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
is a bijection between:

•

the set of integral and graded-polarized variations of mixed Hodge structures on
S
with generic mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and whose associated monodromy representation has image in
\Gamma
;

•

the set of pairs
(\Phi,\rho)
where
\Phi
is a holomorphic horizontal map from
S^{\mathrm{an}}
to
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
and
\rho
is a faithful representation of
\mathbf{P}
.

Definable fundamental domains and algebraic period maps

In general, a mixed Hodge variety
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
is not algebraic but merely a (smooth) complex-analytic variety, and the period map is only a holomorphic map. However in recent years, the use of o-minimal techniques enabled Hodge theorists to bound the transcendence of these analytic objects. We recall the aspects relevant to this work in the present section.

We refer to
[
49
]
for a general introduction to o-minimality and to
[
40
]
and references therein for a general discussion of the applications of o-minimality to Hodge theory. Our main reference for the theory of complex-analytic definable spaces for some o-minimal structure is
[
6
]
. We start with some general recollection on fundamental sets in the o-minimal setting.

Definition 3.24

(
[
5
, Def. 2.1]
)
.

Let
X
be a locally compact Hausdorff topological space, endowed with a definable space structure (in an o-minimal structure) in the sense of
[
49
, Chap. 10, (1.2)]
. Let
\Gamma
be a group acting on
X
by definable homeomorphisms. A
fundamental set
for the action of
\Gamma
on
X
is an open definable subset
\mathscr{F}\subseteq X
such that:

(a)

\Gamma\cdot\mathscr{F}=X
;

(b)

the set
\{\gamma\in\Gamma\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544pt\gamma\cdot\mathscr{F}\cap\mathscr{F}\neq\emptyset\}
is finite.

The existence of such fundamental sets is enough to endow the quotient with a definable structure as the following asserts.

Proposition 3.25

(
[
5
, Prop. 2.3]
)
.

In the setting of Definition
3.24
, if
\mathscr{F}
is a fundamental set for the action of
\Gamma
on
X
, there exists a unique structure of definable space (in the same o-minimal structure) on
\Gamma\backslash X
such that the restriction
\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\Gamma\backslash X
to
\mathscr{F}
of the quotient map is definable.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and
\Gamma
a neat arithmetic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. By Proposition
2.24
(iv)
, the space
D_{\mathbf{P}}
is a connected component of the real semi-algebraic subset
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
of a projective complex algebraic variety, the flag variety
\check{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}
. This endows
D_{\mathbf{P}}
with the structure of a definable complex-analytic space in the o-minimal structure
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{alg}}
. Furthermore elements of
\Gamma
act on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
by
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{alg}}
-definable homeomorphisms, hence we are in the setting of Definition
3.24
.

Theorem 3.26

(
[
5
, Thm. 4.4]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
, let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be its generic mixed Hodge datum, and assume that the image of the monodromy representation associated to
\mathbb{V}
lies in a neat arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma
of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. Let
\Phi:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
be the associated period map. Then,

(i)

there exists an
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{alg}}
-definable fundamental set
\mathscr{F}
for the action of
\Gamma
on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
such that the induced (by Proposition
3.25
) structure of
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{alg}}
-definable space on
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
is functorial for morphisms of mixed Hodge varieties;

(ii)

the map
\Phi
is definable in
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{an,exp}}
for the structure of
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{an,exp}}
-definable space on
S^{\mathrm{an}}
(resp.
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
) extending the structure of
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{alg}}
-definable space coming from its structure of algebraic variety (resp. the structure of
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{alg}}
-definable space defined in
(i)
).

This definability is key for some remarkable algebraization theorems in Hodge theory. We start by recalling the o-minimal Chow theorem of Peterzil-Starchenko (in the restricted generality that we need), which is not specific to Hodge theory but will be used in the sequel.

Theorem 3.27

(
[
42
, Cor. 4.5]
,
[
6
, Thm. 3.3]
)
.

Fix an o-minimal structure. Let
X
be a smooth complex algebraic variety and endow
X^{\mathrm{an}}
with the induced structure of complex-analytic definable space in the fixed o-minimal structure. Let
Y\subset X^{\mathrm{an}}
be a closed complex-analytic and definable subset of
X^{\mathrm{an}}
. Then
Y
is the analytification of an algebraic subset of
X
.

A remarkable application of Theorems
3.26
and
3.27
is the following, which recovers a fundamental result of Cattani-Deligne-Kaplan to be discussed later:

Theorem 3.28

(
[
5
, Cor. 6.7]
)
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass. Then, any complex-analytic irreducible component of
\Phi^{-1}(\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}}))
is an irreducible algebraic subvariety of
S
.

Building on a wide generalization of the result of Peterzil-Starchenko, Bakker-Brunebarbe-Tsimerman proved the following strong algebraicity result, conjectured by Griffiths in the case of pure variations of Hodge structures.

Theorem 3.29

(
[
7
, Thm. 1.1]
)
.

There exists a complex algebraic quasi-projective variety
T
, a closed definable complex-analytic immersion
i:T^{\mathrm{def}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
and a dominant regular morphism
f:S\rightarrow T
such that
\Phi=i^{\mathrm{an}}\circ f^{\mathrm{an}}
.

We will sometimes use the above result through the following corollary:

Corollary 3.30
.

There exists a non-empty Zariski open subset
S^{\prime}
of
S
such that
\Phi((S^{\prime})^{\mathrm{an}})
is a smooth locally closed subset of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
.

Proof.

Let
(T,i,f)
as in Theorem
3.29
. As
f
is dominant
f(S)
contains a Zariski-dense Zariski-open subset
U
of
T
. Let
U^{\mathrm{sm}}
be the smooth locus of
U
and
S^{\prime}=f^{-1}(U^{\mathrm{sm}})
which is a non-empty Zariski open subset of
S
. By construction
\Phi((S^{\prime})^{\mathrm{an}})=i^{\mathrm{an}}((U^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}})
is a smooth locally closed complex analytic subset of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
as desired.
∎

A preparation lemma

The results of this work will be concerned with establishing density (for the Zariski or metric topology) and emptiness results for Hodge loci. The aim of this subsection is to explain that to prove such properties of Hodge loci one can freely replace
S
by a finite étale cover or a non-empty Zariski-open subset, and to collect in a convenient definition all the assumptions that can be made on a mixed variation of Hodge structures up to making changes of
(S,\mathbb{V})
of the type described above.

Lemma 3.31
.

Let
S
be a smooth and irreducible complex algebraic quasi-projective variety. Let
i:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a dominant morphism. Let
\Sigma\subset S
be any subset. Then:

(i)

if
i^{-1}(\Sigma)
is dense in
S^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology, then
\Sigma
is dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology;

(ii)

if
i^{-1}(\Sigma)
is Zariski dense in
S^{\prime}
, then
\Sigma
is Zariski dense in
S
.

In particular, this holds if
i
is a finite étale cover or an open immersion.

Proof.

Assume that
\Sigma
is contained in a strict subset
Z
of
S
closed for the metric (resp. Zariski) topology. Then
i^{-1}(\Sigma)
is contained in
i^{-1}(Z)
which is a closed subset of
S^{\prime}
(for the same topology). One simply has to argue it is a strict subset. Since
i
is dominant and
S
is irreducible (hence a non-empty Zariski open subset of
S
is dense in
S(\mathbb{C})
for both the metric and the Zariski topology) one has
Z\cap i(S^{\prime})\subsetneq i(S^{\prime})
hence
i^{-1}(i(S^{\prime})-Z)\neq\emptyset
. On the other hand, one tautologically has
i^{-1}(Z)\cap i^{-1}(i(S^{\prime})-Z)=\emptyset
. This shows that
i^{-1}(Z)\subsetneq S^{\prime}
.
∎

Definition 3.32
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an integral variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective variety
S
. We say that
\mathbb{V}
is
effective
if the following hold:

(a)

\mathbb{V}
is graded-polarized;

(b)

\mathbb{V}
is admissible;

(c)

the image of the monodromy representation of
\mathbb{V}
at
s
is contained in a neat arithmetic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{s}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
for some (equivalently any)
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
;

(d)

the image of the period map of
\mathbb{V}
is a smooth locally closed complex-analytic subset of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
.

Let
S
be a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective variety and
\mathbb{V}
a variation of mixed Hodge structures on
S
. Let
S^{\prime}\subset S
(resp.
\pi:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
) be a non-empty Zariski open subset (resp. a finite étale cover). Then the local system
\mathbb{V}|_{S^{\prime}}
(resp.
\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
) is easily seen to still carry a variation of mixed Hodge structures. In the sequel, by "up to replacing
S
by a non-empty Zariski open subset
S^{\prime}
" we will mean replacing
(S,\mathbb{V})
by
(S^{\prime},\mathbb{V}|_{S^{\prime}})
. By "up to replacing
S
by a finite étale cover
\pi:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
", we will mean replacing
(S,\mathbb{V})
by
(S^{\prime},\pi^{\ast}\mathbb{V})
.

Proposition 3.33
.

Let
S
be a smooth and irreducible complex quasi-projective variety and
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures on
S
. After successively replacing finitely many times
S
by non-empty Zariski-open subsets or finite étale covers, the variation
\mathbb{V}
can be made effective.

Proof.

Let
(S,\mathbb{V})
be as in the statement. The properties of being admissible and graded-polarized are preserved after passing to non-empty Zariski-open subset (
[
33
, Lem. 3.5]
) or to a finite étale cover (obvious from the definition as axioms
(A_{1})
and
(A_{2})
are insensitive to finite étale covers). We are therefore tasked with proving that properties
(c)
and
(d)
can be achieved after passing to a finite étale cover or to a non-empty Zariski-open subset. For
(c)
, this is precisely the content of Corollary
3.12
. For
(d)
this is Corollary
3.30
.
∎

The pushforward variation

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an effective integral variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
, denote by
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
its generic mixed Hodge class and
\Phi:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
its period map. By Theorem
3.29
, there exists a quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
Y
, a dominant morphism
f:S\rightarrow Y
and a definable closed complex-analytic immersion
i:Y^{\mathrm{def}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}^{\mathrm{def}}
such that
\Phi=i^{\mathrm{an}}\circ f^{\mathrm{an}}
. Since
\mathbb{V}
is effective and
i^{\mathrm{an}}
is a closed complex-analytic immersion, one finds that
Y_{0}:=f^{\mathrm{an}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})=(i^{\mathrm{an}})^{-1}(\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}}))
is a smooth locally-closed complex-analytic subset of
Y^{\mathrm{an}}
, hence a smooth quasi-projective variety. Denote
\Phi_{0}=i^{\mathrm{an}}|_{Y_{0}}:Y_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
.

Lemma 3.34
.

The map
\Phi_{0}
is the period map of an effective integral variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}_{0}
on
Y_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\rho:\mathbf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{GL}(V)
be a faithful representation associated with a point
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
. By Proposition
3.23
the pair
(\Phi_{0},\rho)
defines an integral and graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}_{0}
on
Y_{0}
with normalized period map
\Phi_{0}
. We need to prove that
\mathbb{V}_{0}
is admissible. Let
\overline{Y_{0}}
(resp.
\overline{S}
) be log-smooth compactifications of
Y_{0}
and
S
such that
f:S\rightarrow Y_{0}
extends as
\bar{f}:\overline{S}\rightarrow\overline{Y_{0}}
(such compactifications exist, see the proof of
[
33
, Lem. 3.5]
for instance). Let
j:\Delta\rightarrow\overline{Y_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}
be a holomorphic map such that
j(\Delta^{\times})\subset(Y_{0})^{\mathrm{an}}
and
j^{\ast}\mathbb{V}_{0}
has unipotent monodromy. Since
f
surjects
S
onto
Y_{0}
, there exists an integer
k\geqslant 1
such that denoting by
\pi_{k}:\Delta^{\times}\rightarrow\Delta^{\times},z\mapsto z^{k}
the corresponding cover of
\Delta^{\times}
, the map
j
admits (up to shrinking
\Delta
and restricting
j
to avoid the branch locus of
f
) a lift
\tilde{j}:\Delta\rightarrow\overline{S}^{\mathrm{an}}
which fits in a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.04788pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.54169pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.29169pt\raise 6.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{j}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.33339pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.04788pt\raise-16.23112pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{k}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.33339pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S^{\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.13895pt\raise-16.23112pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.13895pt\raise-24.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-10.54169pt\raise-32.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.21269pt\raise-38.45876pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.54169pt\raise-32.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 34.54169pt\raise-32.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(Y_{0})^{\mathrm{an}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since by construction of
\mathbb{V}_{0}
one has
\mathbb{V}=(f^{\mathrm{an}})^{\ast}\mathbb{V}_{0}
, the commutativity of the above diagram shows that
\tilde{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{V}=(\pi_{k})^{\ast}(j^{\ast}\mathbb{V}_{0})
. By assumption
j^{\ast}\mathbb{V}_{0}
has unipotent monodromy hence the latter identity ensures that
\tilde{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
has unipotent monodromy. It then follows from the admissibility of
\mathbb{V}
that
\tilde{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{V}
is admissible. As properties
(A_{1})
and
(A_{2})
are insensitive to finite étale covers, it follows that
j^{\ast}\mathbb{V}_{0}
is admissible. This finishes the proof.
∎

Hodge loci as intersection loci

The aim of this section is to recall the realization made in
[
39
]
of the Hodge locus as union of intersection loci, so-called special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
and the notion of (a)typicality introduced in
[
10
]
generalizing
[
9
]
.

The Hodge locus

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an integral, graded-polarized and admissible variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth irreducible quasi-projective complex variety
S
. Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be the generic mixed Hodge class of
(S,\mathbb{V})
. The
Hodge locus (of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
)
is the subset

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})=\{s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{s}\subsetneq\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}_{s}^{\otimes})\}=\{s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544pt\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subsetneq\mathbf{P}_{s}\}

of
S
.
The following is immediate from the definitions:

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
i:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be either an open immersion or a finite étale cover. Then
\mathrm{HL}(S^{\prime},(i^{\ast}\mathbb{V})^{\otimes})=i^{-1}(\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes}))
.

Combining the latter to Lemma
3.31
and Proposition
3.33
, one can always assume that
\mathbb{V}
is effective in order to prove (complex-analytic or Zariski) density results of the full Hodge locus or of the Hodge locus of some type.

Mixed Hodge classes of subvarieties

Let
Z\subsetneq S
be a strict irreducible subvariety and denote by
Z^{\mathrm{sm}}
its smooth locus.

Construction

Definition 4.2
.

The generic mixed Hodge class of
Z
is the generic mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
of
\mathbb{V}|_{Z^{\mathrm{sm}}}
.

Let
s_{0}\in Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
. One has an inclusion
i_{Z,s_{0}}:\mathbf{P}_{Z,s_{0}}\subset\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}
and the following is immediate from the construction of the generic mixed Hodge datum of a variation at some base point.

Lemma 4.3
.

The inclusion
i_{Z,s_{0}}
realizes
(\mathbf{P}_{Z,s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s_{0}}})
as a mixed Hodge subdatum of
(\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}})
, and the realization of
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
as a mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
does not depend on the choice of
s_{0}
.

From now on, we identify
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
as a mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
via the inclusion described in the previous lemma.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
Z
be an irreducible subvariety of
S
. Then
\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{an}})\subset\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}})

Proof.

As
\Phi^{-1}(\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}}))
is a closed complex-analytic subset of
S^{\mathrm{an}}
, it suffices to prove that it contains
Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
. Let
s_{0}\in Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
, let
\pi_{Z}:\widetilde{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}
be the universal cover of
(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}
, let
\tilde{s}_{0}
be a lift of
s_{0}
to
\widetilde{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}}
and let
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
. From the constructions, one has the following commutative diagram in the complex-analytic category:

\textstyle{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\widetilde{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\Phi}_{Z,\tilde{s}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{i}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s_{0}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{i}(\tilde{s}_{0})}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{S}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{s_{0}}}

where we denote by
i:(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{an}}
the inclusion, by
\tilde{i}:\widetilde{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
the natural (complex-analytic) map (which set theoretically maps the homotopy class of a continuous path in
(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}
based at
s_{0}
to the homotopy class of the same path seen as a continuous path in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
), by
\tilde{\Phi}_{Z,\tilde{s}_{0}}
the lifted period map of
\mathbb{V}|_{Z^{\mathrm{sm}}}
based at
\tilde{s}_{0}
and by
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{i}(\tilde{s}_{0})}
is the lifted period map of
\mathbb{V}
based at
\tilde{i}(\tilde{s}_{0})
. The triangle on the right consists of the obvious inclusions. The commutativity of the right square ensures that
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{i}(\tilde{s}_{0})}(\tilde{i}(\widetilde{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}}))
is contained in
D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s_{0}}}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}_{s_{0}}}
. The commutativity of the left square ensures that
\pi_{S}(\tilde{i}(\widetilde{(Z^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{an}}}))=Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
. Combining the two shows that
\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C}))\subset\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}})
as desired.
∎

Functoriality

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
Z\subset Z^{\prime}
be two nested irreducible subvarieties of
S
. Then
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})\subseteq(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})
as mixed Hodge subclasses of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

The only non-trivial case is when
Z\subset Z^{\prime}-(Z^{\prime})^{\mathrm{sm}}
, because then there is no easily comparable representatives of
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
and
(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})
. This motivates the following construction.

Let
Z
be an irreducible subvariety of
S
. For
s\in Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
denote by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{Z,s}
the graded vector space
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}((\mathbb{V}|_{Z^{\mathrm{sm}}})^{\otimes})_{s}
of generic Hodge tensors at
s
for
\mathbb{V}|_{Z^{\mathrm{sm}}}
as defined in Sect. 3.2. By definition,
\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}
is the fixator in
\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
of tensors in
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{Z,s}
. For a singular point
s\in Z(\mathbb{C})-Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
, choose a simply-connected open neighborhood
U
of
s
in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
and some
s^{\prime}\in U\cap Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
. Let
\tau
be a continuous path in
U
from
s^{\prime}
to
s
(there is a unique homotopy class of such paths). Define
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{Z,s}
as the parallel transport of
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{Z,s^{\prime}}
along
\tau
. One easily checks that this doesn’t depend on the choice of
U
and
s^{\prime}
. Let
\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}
be the fixator in
\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
of tensors in
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{Z,s}
. By construction, for
U
,
s^{\prime}\in Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})\cap U
and
\tau
as above, the isomorphism
\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}}:\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}})\cong\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
given by parallel transport along
\tau
induces an isomorphism
\mathbf{P}_{Z,s^{\prime}}\cong\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}
which is independent of the above choices.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
. Then
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subset\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\subset\mathbf{P}_{s}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathbb{V}_{s})
. Furthermore, there exists a point
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
such that the inclusion
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}
is an equality.

Proof.

The claimed chain of inclusions is equivalent to

\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{s}\subset\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{Z,s}\subset\mathrm{Hdg}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{V}_{s}^{\otimes})

The first inclusion is true by definition. The second one is a closed condition on
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
(it amounts to asking that some tensors are Hodge) and is true by definition for
s\in Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
. As
Z
is irreducible the subset
Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})
is dense in
Z^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology, and the first assertion follows.

The second part of the statement is Lemma
3.7
applied to
\mathbb{V}|_{Z^{\mathrm{sm}}}
, using that
Z^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{C})\neq\emptyset
.
∎

Therefore, for
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
, the first inclusion shows that the canonical bigraduation of
(\mathbb{V}_{s},(\mathbb{W}_{\bullet})_{s},\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}_{s})
factors through
(\mathbf{P}_{Z,s})_{\mathbb{C}}
and denoting
(\mathbf{P}_{Z,s},X_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}})
the associated mixed Hodge datum, we have proved so far the following generalization of Lemma
4.3
to singular points:

Lemma 4.7
.

For any
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
, the mixed Hodge subdatum
(\mathbf{P}_{Z,s},X_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}})
represents
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
and the inclusion
i_{Z,s}:\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{s}
realizes the inclusion of mixed Hodge classes
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Let
Z\subset Z^{\prime}
be nested irreducible subvarieties of
S
. We need to prove that for some
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
, one has the inclusion
\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\subset\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}
as subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
. Let
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})=\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}
(it exists by Lemma
4.6
). Since
s\in Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
, we find that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subset\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}
. Combining both gives the desired result.
∎

Special subvarieties

Definition 4.8
.

A strict irreducible subvariety
Z
of
S
is a
special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
if for any intermediate (for the inclusion) irreducible subvariety
Z\subsetneq Z^{\prime}\subset S
, the inclusion

(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})\subseteq(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})

of mixed Hodge subclasses of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is strict.

The link between special subvarieties and the Hodge locus of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
relies on the following fundamental result, first proven in
[
18
]
and reproven recently independently in
[
5
]
(see Theorem
3.28
):

Theorem 4.9

(
[
18
, Thm. 1]
)
.

The Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
is a countable union of irreducible algebraic subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
.

Corollary 4.10
.

The Hodge locus
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
is the union of special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
.

Proof.

Let
Z\subset S
be a special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. Let
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
. Applying the definition of special subvarieties to
Z^{\prime}=S
, one finds that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subset\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\subsetneq\mathbf{P}_{s}
. It follows that
s\in\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
.

Conversely, by Theorem
4.9
the Hodge locus is a countable union of irreducible algebraic subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. Let
Z
be any of these. We are tasked with proving that
Z
is contained in a special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. If it is itself a special subvariety, there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, there is some
Z\subsetneq Z^{\prime}\subset S
such that for any
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
one has
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})=(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})
as mixed Hodge subdata of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Since
Z\subset\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
, one has for
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, hence
(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Therefore, for any
s\in Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
, one has
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})\subset\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}\subsetneq\mathbf{P}_{s}
hence
Z^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})
. One can repeat this process with
Z
replaced by
Z^{\prime}
and so on. The process ends because
S
is noetherian, and outputs a special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
containing
Z
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Thereby, the study of the Hodge locus boils down to that of special subvarieties. The latter have a nice characterization formulated by Klingler in
[
39
]
, which was the main motivation of all the work we have done so far. Until the end of this subsection, we assume that
\mathbb{V}
is effective, denote by
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
the generic mixed Hodge class of
\mathbb{V}
and
\Phi:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}
the period map of
\mathbb{V}
, where
\Gamma\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
is a neat arithmetic subgroup containing the image of the monodromy representation.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
Z\subsetneq S
be a strict irreducible subvariety. Then,
Z
is a special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
if and only if
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
is a strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
Z^{\mathrm{an}}
is a complex-analytic irreducible component of
\Phi^{-1}(\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}}))
.

Proof.

Assume that
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
is a strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and that
Z^{\mathrm{an}}
is a complex-analytic irreducible component of
\Phi^{-1}(\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}}))
. Let
Z\subset Z^{\prime}\subset S
be an intermediate irreducible subvariety such that
(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})=(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})
. By Lemma
4.4
, it follows that
\Phi(Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}})\subset\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}})
, hence
Z=Z^{\prime}
by the assumption that
Z^{\mathrm{an}}
is an irreducible component of
\Phi^{-1}(\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}})
. This proves that
Z
is special and thereby the "only if" part of the statement.

Conversely, assume that
Z
is special. This readily forces
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Let
Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
be a complex-analytic irreducible component of
\Phi^{-1}(\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}})
containing
Z^{\mathrm{an}}
. Theorem
3.28
guarantees that
Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
is the analytification of an irreducible algebraic subvariety
Z^{\prime}
of
S
(hence the notation). We want to prove that
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})=(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})
. Let
s\in Z^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s})=\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}
, whose existence is guaranteed by Lemma
4.6
. By the proof of Lemma
4.5
, the inclusion
i_{Z,s}:\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{s}
factors through
i_{Z^{\prime},s}:\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{s}
, resulting in an inclusion
i_{Z,Z^{\prime},s}:\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}
as subgroups of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
. Furthermore
i_{Z,Z^{\prime},s}
induces an inclusion of mixed Hodge data
(\mathbf{P}_{Z,s},X_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}})\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s},X_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}})
as mixed Hodge subdata of
(\mathbf{P}_{s},X_{\mathbf{P}_{s}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{s}})
. Therefore, one has an inclusion
(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}})\subseteq(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime}}})
as mixed Hodge subclasses of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, and to prove that this inclusion is an equality, it suffices to prove that
i_{Z,Z^{\prime},s}:\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}
is an equality. To see this, let
s^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
be a point such that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}})=\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s^{\prime}}
(again, it is possible by Lemma
4.6
). Let
\tau:[0,1]\rightarrow Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}}
be a continuous path with
\tau(0)=s
and
\tau(1)=s^{\prime}
. Let
\tilde{s}\in\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
be a lift of
s
to the universal cover, and
\tilde{s^{\prime}}
be the lift of
s^{\prime}
to
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
which is the endpoint of the canonical lift of
\tau
to
\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}}
be the lifted period map based at
\tilde{s}
. Since
\Phi(Z^{\prime\mathrm{an}})\subset\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}_{Z},D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}),\Gamma_{\mathbf{P}_{Z}}})
, there exists a
\gamma\in\Gamma
such that
\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime})\in\gamma\cdot D_{\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}_{s}}
. From the construction of the lifted period map, and Proposition
2.37
one finds:

(\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}})^{-1}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}}))=\mathbf{P}(\tilde{\Phi}_{\tilde{s}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}))\subset\gamma\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\gamma^{-1},

as subgroups of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
. As we chose
s^{\prime}
such that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{V}_{s^{\prime}})=\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s^{\prime}}
, and since by construction
(\tau_{\ast}^{\mathbf{GL}})^{-1}(\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s^{\prime}})=\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}
, we find:

\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\subset\mathbf{P}_{Z^{\prime},s}\subset\gamma\mathbf{P}_{Z,s}\gamma^{-1}

as subgroups of
\mathbf{P}_{s}
. The first and last members of the latter chain of inequality have the same dimension, and all members are connected
\mathbb{Q}
