x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t^{\prime})
. By definition of
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}
, one has that
\dim_{x}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)=\dim_{x^{\prime}}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t^{\prime})=C_{\mathbf{N}}
. One then concludes as in the proof of
(a)
that
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is equidimensional with dimension independent of the choice of
(\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}},D_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
-intersecting pair.

We are therefore left with proving that
S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
is a strict Zariski-closed subset of
S
. By definition,
S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
is a strict subset of
S
. Recall that
\Phi
factors as
i\circ f
where
f:S\rightarrow Y
is the analytification of a dominant regular morphism to a quasi-projective complex variety and
i:Y^{\mathrm{an}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}
is the analytification of a closed definable immersion. This is summarized in the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\Phi}}
\textstyle{D_{\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\Gamma}}
\textstyle{S^{\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}}

where
\pi_{\Gamma}:D_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
is the quotient map. Therefore, if we let
Y_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}:=i^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}))
, we have
S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}=f^{-1}(Y_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}})
. Hence we are reduced to proving that
Y_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
is Zariski-closed in
Y
. By the definable Chow Theorem
3.27
, it suffices to prove that it is a definable closed complex-analytic subset. It is definable because
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}\subset\mathscr{F}
is a definable subset and
i
and
\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\mathscr{F}}
are definable maps. Denote by
i_{\mathbf{N}}:=p_{\mathbf{N}}\circ i
which is a complex analytic map. We claim that

Y_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}=\{y\in Y^{\mathrm{an}}\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544pt\dim_{y}i_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(i_{\mathbf{N}}(y))>C_{\mathbf{N}}\}.

Because
i_{\mathbf{N}}
is a complex analytic morphism, this would prove that
Y_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
is a closed complex-analytic subset of
Y
. As the projection
\pi_{\Gamma}
is a local isomorphism on the source and
i
is a closed immersion, for
z\in\mathscr{I}
one has

\dim_{z}\mathscr{I}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(q_{\mathbf{N}}(z))=\dim_{\pi_{\Gamma}(z)}i(Y^{\mathrm{an}})\cap p_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(p_{\mathbf{N}}(\pi_{\Gamma}(z)))=\dim_{i^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(z))}i_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(i_{\mathbf{N}}(i^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(z)))).

The above claim follows from the latter fact, the definition of
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
and the fact that
Y_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}=i^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}))
.

The Zariski open subset of
S
whose existence is claimed in the statement shall not depend on
\mathbf{N}
, so we have to get rid of the dependence in
\mathbf{N}
of
S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}
. This is a consequence of Baldi-Urbanik’s geometric Zilber-Pink theorem. Let
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ex}}
be the set of
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
such that there exists a
(\mathscr{I},D_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
-intersecting pair
(g,t)
such that
\mathscr{I}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is either not equidimensional or equidimensional of dimension bigger than the generic dimension
C_{\mathbf{N}}
defined above.

Lemma 7.4
.

The set
\bigcup_{\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ex}}}S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
is a finite union of strict Zariski-closed subsets of
S
.

Assuming this statement for a moment, the intersection
S_{\mathrm{gen}}:=\bigcap_{\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ex}}}S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{gen}}
is then a non-empty Zariski open subset of
S
as it is a finite intersection of non-empty Zariski open subsets of the irreducible complex algebraic variety
S
. Furthermore, by construction
S_{\mathrm{gen}}
satisfies the properties required in the statement.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

Using notations of Theorem
5.11
, let
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ZP}}
be the set of
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
such that
((\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\mathbf{N})\in\Sigma_{(S,\mathbb{V})}
. By
op. cit.
we know that
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ZP}}
is finite, hence it suffices to argue that

\bigcup_{\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ex}}}S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}\subset\bigcup_{\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ZP}}}S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}

Let
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ex}}
and
s\in S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
. By definition, there exists a
(\mathscr{I},D_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
-intersecting pair
(g,t)
and a complex-analytic irreducible component
U
of
\mathscr{I}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
such that
\dim U>C_{\mathbf{N}}
and
s\in\Phi^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(U))
. Since
\mathscr{I}
and
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
are smooth locally closed complex analytic subsets of
D_{\mathbf{H}}
, a combination of Lemma
4.12
and the definition of
C_{\mathbf{N}}
shows that
C_{\mathbf{N}}\geqslant\dim\mathscr{I}+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)-\dim D_{\mathbf{H}}
. Therefore, one finds that any complex-analytic irreducible component of
\Phi^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(U))
is a monodromically atypical weakly special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
in the sense of Definition
5.10
. Summing up, we have shown that
s
is contained in a monodromically atypical weakly special subvariety
Z
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. Let
Z^{\prime}
be a maximal one containing
Z
. By definition of
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ZP}}
one finds that
\mathbf{H}_{Z^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ZP}}
and since
Z^{\prime}
is monodromically atypical, arguing as above shows that
Z^{\prime}\subset S_{\mathbf{H}_{Z^{\prime}},\mathrm{ex}}
. This shows that
s\in\bigcup_{\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})_{\mathrm{ZP}}}S_{\mathbf{N},\mathrm{ex}}
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
a mixed Hodge subclass. Then
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely if and only if for every
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
, one has:

d_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\leqslant d_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}).

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
. Let
S_{\mathrm{gen}}
and
\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}
be as in the statement of Proposition
7.3
. Let
(g,t)
be an
(\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}},D_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
-intersecting pair. Then, because
\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is equidimensional and coïncides with the fiber
(q_{\mathbf{N}}|_{\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}})^{-1}(t)
, we find:

\dim\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}=\dim\Big(\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\Big)+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}).

Similarly,
\dim g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}=\dim\Big((g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\Big)+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
. Therefore, one has:

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbf{M},X_{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})-d_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbf{M},X_{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\dim(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})-\dim\Big((g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\Big[\dim\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}-\dim\Big(\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\Big[\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{gen}})-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim p_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{M}})+\dim\Phi_{\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})-\dim\mathscr{D}/\mathbf{N}.

This immediately implies the claimed equivalence.
∎

Existence of transverse intersections

In this subsection we establish two intermediate results which will be of constant use in the sequel, and at the same time clarify the link between
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness and transverse intersections. The first is:

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
a strict mixed Hodge subclass and assume that there exists an
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety
Z\subset S
, such that
Z\cap S_{\mathrm{gen}}\neq\emptyset
, where
S_{\mathrm{gen}}
is the non-empty Zariski open subset of
S
defined in Proposition
7.3
. Then
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.

Proof.

We use the notations of Proposition
7.3
. First remark that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely if and only if it is
\mathbb{V}|_{S_{\mathrm{gen}}}
-likely. As
Z\cap S_{\mathrm{gen}}
is non-empty, it is an
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety of
S_{\mathrm{gen}}
. Hence we will assume
S=S_{\mathrm{gen}}
. Let
\mathscr{Z}\subset\mathscr{I}\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}
be a complex-analytic irreducible component such that
\pi_{\Gamma}(\mathscr{Z})=\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{an}})
. Since
Z
is transverse, one has

\dim\mathscr{Z}=\dim\mathscr{I}+\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}=d_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}).

Let
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
. Since fibers of
q_{\mathbf{N}}
cover
D_{\mathbf{P}}
, there exists
t\in D/\mathbf{N}
such that
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{Z}\neq\emptyset
. In particular,
(1,t)
is an
(\mathscr{I},D_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
-intersecting pair. Since we assumed
S=S_{\mathrm{gen}}
, one has:

\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I}=\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D

and

\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}=\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)+\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}-\dim D.

Since
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
,
D_{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathscr{I}
are smooth equidimensional locally closed complex-analytic subsets of
D
(for
\mathscr{I}
this is because
\mathbb{V}
was assumed to be effective in this section), the above equalities combined to Lemma
4.12
(iii) ensure that
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I}
and
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}
are smooth (equidimensional) locally closed complex-analytic subsets of
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
. Now remark that each irreducible component
\mathscr{Z}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is a complex-analytic irreducible component of the intersection
(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I})\cap(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}})
in
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
. Hence, by Lemma
4.12
(ii),

\dim\mathscr{Z}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\geqslant\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I}+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}-\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)=d_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})

where the last equality follows from the definition, using that
(1,t)
is an
(\mathscr{I},D_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
-intersecting pair. Finally, as
\dim\mathscr{Z}\geqslant\dim\mathscr{Z}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
, we obtain

d_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})=\dim\mathscr{Z}\geqslant\dim\mathscr{Z}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)=d_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}}).

By Corollary
7.5
, this proves that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.
∎

The second result is:

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
a strict
\mathbb{V}
-likely mixed Hodge subclass. Let
s\in S_{\mathrm{gen}}^{\mathrm{an}}
be a Hodge generic point, let
x=\Phi(s)
and let
z\in\mathscr{I}
such that
\pi_{\Gamma}(z)=x
. Let
g\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
such that
z\in(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})\cap\mathscr{I}
and let
U
be a complex-analytic irreducible component of
(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})\cap\mathscr{I}
containing
z
. Then,

\dim U=\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}.

Proof.

Arguing as at the beginning of the proof of Proposition
7.6
using that
s\in S_{\mathrm{gen}}^{\mathrm{an}}
, we can assume that
S=S_{\mathrm{gen}}
. We proceed in three steps.

Step 1 :
\dim U\geqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Since
\mathbb{V}
is assumed to be effective one has that
\mathscr{I}
is a smooth locally closed complex-analytic subset of
D_{\mathbf{P}}
. Besides
D_{\mathbf{M}}
is a smooth closed complex-analytic subset of
D_{\mathbf{P}}
. By Lemma
4.12
(ii), we have the claimed inequality of dimensions. For the remaining part of the proof, we assume for the sake of contradiction that this inequality is strict, that is:

\dim U>\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}.

Step 2 : There exists a
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
such that
U\subset q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
.

Firstly, remark that since
\tilde{\Phi}(\widetilde{S^{\mathrm{an}}})\lx@nobreakspace\subset D_{\mathbf{H}}
, one has
\mathscr{I}\subset D_{\mathbf{H}}
hence
U\subset D_{\mathbf{H}}
. In particular
\dim\mathscr{I}=\dim\mathscr{I}\cap D_{\mathbf{H}}
and
\dim U=\dim U\cap D_{\mathbf{H}}
. By the reformulation of
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness in Corollary
7.5
with
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{H}
, one finds that

\dim(D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap D_{\mathbf{H}})+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{H}}\leqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}},

which implies

\dim U>\dim(D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap D_{\mathbf{H}})+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{H}}.

Let
Y_{0}
and
\Phi_{0}
be as in Lemma
3.34
. Since
\Phi_{0}
is the period map of an admissible graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures on
Y_{0}
by Lemma
3.34
, the Ax-Schanuel Theorem
5.9
applies to
\Phi_{0}
. Set
W=Y_{0}\times(g\cdot D_{M})\cap D_{\mathbf{H}}
which is an algebraic subset of
Y_{0}\times D_{\mathbf{H}}
, let
\Delta=Y_{0}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}}),\Gamma}}D_{\mathbf{H}}
. Denote by
p_{Y_{0}}
(resp.
p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}
) the projection of
Y_{0}\times D_{\mathbf{H}}
to
Y_{0}
(resp.
D_{\mathbf{H}}
). Set theoretically,

p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(W\cap\Delta)=\tilde{\Phi}(\widetilde{Y_{0}^{\mathrm{an}}})\cap(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}),

which contains
U
. Let
Z\subset\Delta\cap W
a complex-analytic irreducible component such that
U\subset p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(Z)
. In particular
\dim Z\geqslant\dim U
. We claim that
\dim Z>\dim W+\dim\Delta-\dim Y_{0}\times D_{\mathbf{H}}
. Indeed,

\dim W+\dim\Delta-\dim Y_{0}\times D_{\mathbf{H}}=\dim(D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap D_{\mathbf{H}})+\dim\Delta-\dim D_{\mathbf{H}}.

Hence, one has to compute
\dim\Delta
. As
\pi_{\Gamma}
has discrete fibers, the definition of
\Delta
implies that
p_{Y_{0}}|_{\Delta}
has discrete fibers. It follows that
\dim\Delta=\dim Y_{0}=\dim\mathscr{I}
. Therefore,

\dim Z\geqslant\dim U>\dim(D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap D_{\mathbf{H}})+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{H}}=\dim W+\dim\Delta-\dim Y_{0}\times D_{\mathbf{H}}.

By the Ax-Schanuel Theorem
5.9
, there exists a strict weakly special subvariety
X_{0}
of
Y_{0}
for
\mathbb{V}_{0}
containing
p_{Y_{0}}(Z)
. Unwrapping definitions, this implies that there exists a
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
and a
t\in D/\mathbf{N}
such that
\Phi_{0}(p_{Y_{0}}(Z))\subset\pi_{\Gamma}(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t))
. This implies that
\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\Phi_{0}(p_{Y_{0}}(Z)))\subset\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)))
. Since
Z\subset\Delta
, one has that
\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\Phi_{0}(p_{Y_{0}}(Z)))=p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(Z)
, hence the above containement rewrites
p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(Z)\subset\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)))
. On the other hand, since
\mathbf{N}
is normal in
\mathbf{P}
, for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
, the translate
\gamma\cdot q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is the
\mathbf{N}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{N}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
\gamma\cdot h
for some
h\in q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
. Letting
t_{\gamma}=q_{\mathbf{N}}(\gamma\cdot h)
, this rewrites
\gamma\cdot q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)=q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t_{\gamma})
. In particular, for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
the intersection
\gamma\cdot q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is either empty or equal to
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
. Therefore the
\gamma\cdot q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
are the connected components of

\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)))=\bigcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma\cdot q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t).

The fact that
p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(Z)
is connected (because
Z
is) then implies that there exists some
\gamma\in\Gamma
such that
p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(Z)\subset\gamma\cdot q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)=q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t_{\gamma})
. By definition of
Z
, one has
U\subset p_{D_{\mathbf{H}}}(Z)
, hence
U\subset q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t_{\gamma})
. This finishes Step
2
.

Step 3 : Contradiction

Choose
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
of minimal dimension among those satisfying the property that there exists some (necessarily unique as the fibers of
q_{\mathbf{N}}
are disjoint)
t\in D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}
such that
U\subset q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
. Let
X_{0}\subset Y_{0}
be the strict weakly special subvariety
\Phi_{0}^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)))
of
Y_{0}
for
\mathbb{V}_{0}
. We claim that

\dim U=\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I}+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}-\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t).

Indeed, arguing as in the proof of Proposition
7.6
, using that
S=S_{\mathrm{gen}}
, one finds that
U=U\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
is an irreducible complex-analytic component of the intersection between the smooth locally closed complex analytic subsets
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I}
and
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}
of
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
in
q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)
, so that

\dim U\geqslant\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap\mathscr{I}+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\cap D_{\mathbf{M}}-\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t).

If this inequality was to be strict, the use of the Ax-Schanuel Theorem
5.9
exactly as in Step
2
(replacing
Y_{0}
by
X_{0}
and
\Phi_{0}
by
\Phi_{0}|_{X_{0}}
) would produce some
\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
with smaller dimension than
\mathbf{N}
such that
U\subset q_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{-1}(t^{\prime})
for some
t^{\prime}\in D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}^{\prime}
. This would contradict the minimality of
\mathbf{N}
. This proves the claimed equality. We conclude the proof by remarking that the chain of inequalities

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim U-\dim U

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\big[\dim\mathscr{I}+\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}\big]-\big[\dim\mathscr{I}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)+\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}-\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}^{-1}(t)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})-d_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})

contradicts the
\mathbb{V}
-likely property for
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
formulated as in Proposition
7.5
.
∎

Consequences of likeliness and horizontality

In this subsection, we work in the following setting:

Setting 7.8
.

Fix
\mathbb{V}
an effective variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex variety
S
, with notations as in Setting
7.1
. We fix a mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely and a point
z\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
along with some associated bigraduation
x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{M}_{\mathbb{C}}
. We denote by
\mathfrak{m}
(resp.
\mathfrak{p}
) the Lie algebra algebra of
\mathbf{M}
(resp.
\mathbf{P}
) and

\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p+q\leqslant 0}\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p+q\leqslant 0}\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}

the bigraduations associated with
x
and the adjoint representations of
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{M}
. Under the above inclusion, one has
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
for every
p+q\leqslant 0
. We denote by
W_{\bullet}\mathfrak{p}
and
W_{\bullet}\mathfrak{m}
the weight filtrations on
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{m}
. Finally, we denote by
h_{\mathfrak{m}}^{p,q}
(resp.
h_{\mathfrak{p}}^{p,q}
) the complex dimension of
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}
(resp.
\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
).

In this setting, the mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is heavily constrained:

Proposition 7.9
.

The following assertions hold:

(i)

the inclusion
W_{-3}\mathfrak{m}\subset W_{-3}\mathfrak{p}
is an equality;

(ii)

the inclusion
\mathbf{M}/\mathbf{H}\cap\mathbf{M}\subset\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{H}
is an equality;

(iii)

the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
is an equality for every
(p,q)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
p+q\leqslant 0
and
(p,q)\notin\{(-1,1),(1,-1),(0,0),(0,-1),(-1,0),(-1,-1)\}
.

Proof.

Let
h\in\mathscr{I}
be a lift of the image under
\Phi
of some Hodge generic point of
S^{\mathrm{an}}
. As
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
acts transitively on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
, there exists an element
g\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
such that
h=g\cdot z
hence
h\in g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap\mathscr{I}
. Since
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely, Proposition
7.7
ensures that there exists a complex irreducible component of
(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})\cap\mathscr{I}
containing
x
along which
g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathscr{I}
intersect transversally. By Proposition
4.14
, this implies that

T_{h}(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})+T_{h}\mathscr{I}=T_{h}D_{\mathbf{P}}.

Let
\mathrm{Ad}:\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}})
be the adjoint representation. The bigraduation
\mathrm{int}_{g}\circ x
satisfies Pink’s axioms and defines a bigraduation of
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
. Therefore, the tangent space
T_{h}D_{\mathbf{P}}
naturally identifies to

\bigoplus_{p+q\leqslant 0,p<0}\mathrm{Ad}(g)(\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}).

Under this identification
T_{h}(g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
identifies to

\bigoplus_{p+q\leqslant 0,p<0}\mathrm{Ad}(g)(\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}),

and one has

T_{h}\mathscr{I}\subset\bigoplus_{q\leqslant 1}\mathrm{Ad}(g)(\mathfrak{p}^{-1,q})

by Griffiths transversality. In particular, this implies that for
p+q\leqslant-1
and
p<-1
the inclusion
\mathrm{Ad}(g)(\mathfrak{m}^{p,q})\subset\mathrm{Ad}(g)(\mathfrak{p}^{p,q})
is an equality hence
h_{\mathfrak{m}}^{p,q}=h_{\mathfrak{p}}^{p,q}
as
\mathrm{Ad}(g)
preserves complex dimensions. In turn, one finds that for
p+q\leqslant-1
and
p<-1
, the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
is an equality. This implies both assertions
(i)
and
(iii)
as we shall now explain.

For assertion
(i)
, let
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
p+q\leqslant-3
. If
p<-1
, there is nothing to prove. Assume that
p\geqslant-1
, which implies
q\leqslant-2
. By Lemma
2.9
, Hodge symmetry holds at the level of Hodge numbers, hence the equality
h_{\mathfrak{m}}^{q,p}=h_{\mathfrak{p}}^{q,p}
(which holds because
q<-1
) implies that
h_{\mathfrak{m}}^{p,q}=h_{\mathfrak{p}}^{p,q}
which in turn implies
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}=\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
as desired. By Equation
2.1.3
, this implies that
W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})=W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})
as desired.

For assertion
(iii)
, let
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
p+q\leqslant 0
. If
p+q\leqslant-3
, the statement is already a consequence of
(i)
. Otherwise, by Lemma
2.9
one has
h_{\mathfrak{m}}^{p,q}=h_{\mathfrak{p}}^{p,q}
as soon as
q<-1
or
p<-1
. Assertion
(iii)
follows.

We are left with proving assertion
(ii)
. Applying the
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness condition relative to the algebraic monodromy group
\mathbf{H}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
, one finds that

\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{H}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})+\dim q_{\mathbf{H}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})\geqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{H}.

By Lemma
5.7
, one has
\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{H}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})=0
and the above inequality shows that
q_{\mathbf{H}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})=D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{H}
. Let
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
such that
\mathbf{P}(h)=\mathbf{P}
, for instance one can take the lift in
\mathscr{I}
of the image under
\Phi
of a Hodge generic point in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
. Then
\mathbf{P}(q_{\mathbf{H}}(h))=\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{H}
. Since
q_{\mathbf{H}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})=D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{H}
it follows that
q_{\mathbf{H}}(h)\in q_{\mathbf{H}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})
hence
\mathbf{P}(q_{\mathbf{H}}(h))\subset\mathbf{M}/(\mathbf{M}\cap\mathbf{H})
. The other inclusion holds trivially hence
\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{M}/\mathbf{M}\cap\mathbf{H}
.
∎

The transverse mixed Hodge locus of prescribed type

All-or-nothing

The aim of this subsection is to prove the following:

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible integral and graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass of the generic mixed Hodge class
(\mathbf{P},D)
of
\mathbb{V}
. Assume that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty. Then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S
for the Zariski topology.

Proof.

Zariski-density of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
remains unchanged if one pulls-back
(S,\mathbb{V})
along finite etale covers or open immersions. Besides, in the proof of Proposition
3.33
, the only step at which one needs replacing
S
by a non-empty Zariski open subset of
S
is to restrict to the smooth locus of
\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
which preserves the non-emptiness of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
, since the image of a transverse special subvariety under the period map intersects the smooth locus of
\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
by definition. Hence, we can assume in the proof that
\mathbb{V}
is effective, which we do starting from now, and work in Setting
7.1

Assume that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}\neq\emptyset
. Up to replacing
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
by a rational conjugate (which doesn’t change
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
), we can assume that there exists an
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety
Z
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. Let
S_{\mathbf{M}}
be the Zariski-closure of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
in
S
. We want to prove that
S_{\mathbf{M}}=S
.

We first claim that it suffices to prove that
\Phi(S_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathrm{an}})=\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
. Indeed assume for the sake of contradiction that the latter holds but
S_{\mathbf{M}}\subsetneq S
. Recall that
\Phi
factors as
i^{\mathrm{an}}\circ f^{\mathrm{an}}
with
f
a dominant algebraic morphism from
S
to a quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
Y
, and
i
a definable immersion. Since
f(S_{\mathbf{M}})=f(S)
we know that
S_{\mathbf{M}}
meets every fiber of
f
over
f(S)
, and since
S_{\mathbf{M}}\subsetneq S
, generic flatness applied to
f|_{S_{\mathbf{M}}}
ensures the existence of a dense Zariski-open subset
U\subset f(S_{\mathbf{M}})
such that
S_{\mathbf{M}}
meets the fiber of
f
over any point of
U
properly. By definition of
S_{\mathbf{M}}
, the preimages by
i
of transverse irreducible components of intersections of
\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
with
\phi_{i,\Gamma}(\mathscr{D}_{(g\mathbf{M}g^{-1},g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}),\Gamma_{g\mathbf{M}g^{-1}}})
(for
g
varying in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
) are Zariski dense in
f(S_{\mathbf{M}})
, hence we can pick one, say
X\subset f(S_{\mathbf{M}})
, meeting
U
. But now by definition, the fiber over any point of
X\cap U
is fully contained in
S_{\mathbf{M}}
(because special subvarieties of
S
are preimages under
\Phi
of intersections of the period image with Hodge subvarieties) which gives the desired contradiction.

Let
x\in\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{an}})
be a smooth point, and
z\in\mathscr{I}
such that
\pi_{\Gamma}(z)=x
. Since
\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
is irreducible it suffices to prove that
T_{x}\Phi(S_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathrm{an}})=T_{x}\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
. Let
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}=\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\Phi(S_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathrm{an}}))\cap\mathscr{I}
, and
\mathscr{Z}\subset\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}
a complex-analytic irreducible component of the intersection of
\mathscr{I}
and
D_{\mathbf{M}}
containing
z
, such that
\pi_{\Gamma}
identifies the germ of
\mathscr{Z}
at
z
with the germ of
\Phi(Z^{\mathrm{an}})
at
x
. Since
\pi_{\Gamma}
is a local biholomorphism, the equality
T_{x}\Phi(S_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathrm{an}})=T_{x}\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})
is equivalent to
T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{z}\mathscr{I}
in
T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}
. Recall that
T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}
can be identified to
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})/F_{z}^{0}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})
. Denote by
(T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}
the image of
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{R}})
under the quotient map
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})/F_{z}^{0}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbb{C}})
. We will use the following, to be shown below.

Lemma 8.2
.

(T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}\subset T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}+T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}\subset T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}

Then, combining the facts that
(T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}\oplus i(T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}=T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}
and that
T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}+T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}
is a complex vector subspace of
T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}
, one finds that
T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}+T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}
. On the other hand, by definition
\mathscr{I}
and
D_{\mathbf{M}}
intersect transversally along
\mathscr{Z}
, so that
T_{z}\mathscr{I}+T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}
and
T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}\cap T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{z}\mathscr{Z}
. But
\mathscr{Z}\subset\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}
hence
T_{z}\mathscr{Z}\subset T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}
, so that
T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}\cap T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{z}\mathscr{Z}
. Therefore, using Grassmann formula twice, one finds

\dim T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}=\dim T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}}-\dim T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim T_{z}\mathscr{Z}=\dim T_{z}\mathscr{I}.

This shows that
T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{z}\mathscr{I}
as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.2
.

Let
\mu:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\times D_{\mathbf{P}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}},(p,x)\mapsto p\cdot x
be the action map, and for any
x\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
denote by
\mu_{x}:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
be the composition of
\mu
with the real-analytic immersion
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\times D_{\mathbf{P}},p\mapsto(p,x)
. By definition, one has that
(T_{x}D_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(d_{1}\mu_{x})
, where
1
denotes the identity of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
.

By assumption,

\dim_{z}\mathscr{Z}=\dim_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim_{z}\mathscr{I}-\dim_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}

hence by Lemma
4.14
the inclusion
i_{1}:D_{\mathbf{M}}\hookrightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
is transverse to
\mathscr{I}
at
z
. The map
i:=\mu|_{\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\times D_{\mathbf{M}}}:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\times D_{\mathbf{M}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
is a smooth map such that for any
x\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
, one has
i(1,x)=i_{1}(x)
. Therefore, Theorem
4.15
ensures the existence of an open neighborhood
\Omega
of
1
in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
and a smooth map
\sigma:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{M}}
such that
\sigma(1)=z
and for any
g\in\Omega
, the map
i_{g}:=i(g,\cdot):D_{\mathbf{M}}\hookrightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
is transverse to
\mathscr{I}
at
\sigma(g)
.

Let
\mathscr{X}=i^{-1}(\mathscr{I})\cap\pi^{-1}(\Omega)
where
\pi:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\times D_{\mathbf{M}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
is the projection map. We still denote by
\pi
the restriction of the projection to
\mathscr{X}
and we denote by
f:\mathscr{X}\rightarrow\mathscr{I}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}
the restriction to
\mathscr{X}
of
i
. These objects fit in the following diagram of smooth manifolds.

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.81944pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.41107pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.81944pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.67632pt\raise-15.94888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.43552pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.81944pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{I}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-17.81944pt\raise-31.89778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 56.50623pt\raise-31.89778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We claim that the following two properties are satisfied:

(1)

the smooth map
\sigma_{\mathbf{M}}:\Omega\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
which assigns to a
g\in\Omega
the point
\mu(g^{-1},\sigma(g))
has image in
D_{\mathbf{M}}
;

(2)

the smooth map
\sigma
has image in
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}\subset\mathscr{I}
.

Property
(1)
is simply a consequence of the definition of
\mathscr{X}
. Indeed for any
(g,x)\in\mathscr{X}
, one has
x\in g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}\cap\mathscr{I}
hence
\mu(g^{-1},x)\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
. Furthermore,
\sigma_{\mathbf{M}}
is a smooth map as
\mu
,
\sigma
and the inversion in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
are. To show property
(2)
, note that by construction
g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathscr{I}
intersect transversally at
\sigma(g)
for any
g\in\Omega
. Hence, for any
g\in\Omega\cap\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
, one has
\pi_{\Gamma}(\sigma(g))\in\Phi(S_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathrm{an}})
and therefore
\sigma(g)\in\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}
. Since
\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}
is locally closed and
\Omega\cap\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
is dense in
\Omega
, it follows that
\sigma(g)\in\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}
for any
g\in\Omega
as claimed in
(2)
.

We are now in position to conclude the proof. By definition of
\sigma_{\mathbf{M}}
, we have
\sigma(g)=\mu(g,\sigma_{\mathbf{M}}(g))
for any
g\in\Omega
. Applying the chain rule, we therefore have:

d_{1}\sigma=(d_{(1,z)}\mu)\circ(\mathrm{id}\oplus d_{1}\sigma_{\mathbf{M}})=d_{1}\mu_{z}+d_{1}\sigma_{\mathbf{M}}.

Therefore,

(T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}})_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(d_{1}\mu_{z})\subset\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(d_{1}\sigma-d_{1}\sigma_{\mathbf{M}})\subset T_{z}\mathscr{I}_{\mathbf{M}}+T_{z}D_{\mathbf{M}}\subset T_{z}D_{\mathbf{P}},

as desired.
∎

Corollary 8.3
.

Assume that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty. Then
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty, Theorem
8.1
ensures that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is Zariski-dense in
S
. Therefore, there exists a
g\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
and a
(g\mathbf{M}g^{-1},g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety
Z
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
such that
Z\cap S_{\mathrm{gen}}\neq\emptyset
. By Proposition
7.6
, this forces
(g\mathbf{M}g^{-1},g\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
to be
\mathbb{V}
-likely, which in turn implies that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.
∎

Finally, we can readily prove Corollary
1.6
:

Proof of Corollary
1.6
.

Assume that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ},\mathrm{pos}}
is non-empty. In particular there exists a strict mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
such that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty and:

\dim D_{\mathbf{M}}+\dim\Phi(S^{\mathrm{an}})-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}>0.

By Theorem
8.1
, the non-emptiness of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
implies that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S
for the Zariski-topology. Besides the dimension inequality above implies that

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}\subset\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{pos}}

. Hence we have shown that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{pos}}
is dense in
S
for the metric topology. On the other hand, Corollary
5.14
implies that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp},\mathrm{pos}}
is not dense in
S
for the Zariski topology since
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp},\mathrm{pos}}
is contained in
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp},\mathrm{f-pos}}
by definition. Combining the above two facts, the disjunction
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{pos}}=\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ},\mathrm{pos}}\cup\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{atyp},\mathrm{pos}}
forces
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ},\mathrm{pos}}
to be dense in
S
for the Zariski topology, as desired.
∎

Analytic density

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and denote by
W_{\bullet}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
the induced weight filtration on
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
.

Definition 8.4
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. It is called
sufficiently unipotent
if
\exp(W_{-2}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))\subset\mathbf{M}
.

The usefulness of this a priori obscure property lies in the following:

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
which is sufficiently unipotent. Then
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}=D_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Proof.

Choose
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{M},X_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
representatives of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
and
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
such that the inclusion of
\mathbf{M}
in
\mathbf{P}
makes
(\mathbf{M},X_{\mathbf{M}},D_{\mathbf{M}})
a mixed Hodge subdatum of
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
. Let
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}:=\exp(W_{-2}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))
. Let
X_{\mathbf{P}}^{\prime}
be the set of
x\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
such that the post-composite of
x
with the complexification of the quotient map
\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
. By
[
43
, Prop. 1.16]
, we have that
X_{\mathbf{P}}^{\prime}
is a non-empty
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
X_{\mathbf{P}}
such that
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}(X_{\mathbf{P}}^{\prime})=\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{P}}
. Since
\psi_{X_{\mathbf{P}}}
is
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant, this proves that

\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(\mathbb{C})=(\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C}))\cap(\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(\mathbb{C}))\subset\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{P}^{u}(\mathbb{C})

acts transitively on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Assume that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is sufficiently unipotent, that is
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{M}
. By definition of
D_{\mathbf{P}}
, one has the containment
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}D_{\mathbf{M}}\subset D_{\mathbf{P}}
, so we are tasked with proving the inverse inclusion. Let
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
. Since
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(\mathbb{C})
acts transitively on
D_{\mathbf{P}}
, there exists
h_{\mathbf{M}}\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
,
p\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
and
u\in\mathbf{U}^{\prime}(\mathbb{C})
such that
pu\cdot h_{\mathbf{M}}=h
. From weight considerations one has that
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{M}
is a unipotent normal subgroup of
\mathbf{M}
. Therefore it is contained in
\mathbf{M}^{u}
and we find
u\cdot h_{\mathbf{M}}\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
. Therefore
h\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}
as desired.
∎

Then the main result of this subsection can be stated as follows:

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible integral and graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth irreducible and quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be the generic Hodge class of
\mathbb{V}
. Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
which is sufficiently unipotent. Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty;

(ii)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology;

(iii)

(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.

Proof.

Of course
(ii)
implies
(i)
, and Corollary
8.3
shows that
(i)
implies
(iii)
, both without assuming that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is sufficiently unipotent. Assume that
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely and sufficiently unipotent. We are tasked with proving that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology. As the assumptions and desired conclusion are unchanged after replacing
S
by a non-empty Zariski-open subset or by a finite etale cover, we can assume that
\mathbb{V}
is effective and work in Setting
7.1
, and assume that
S=S_{\mathrm{gen}}
in the notation of Proposition
7.3
.

Let
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
be a Hodge generic point, and
z\in\mathscr{I}
a lift of
\Phi(s)
in
\mathscr{I}
. let
B_{0}\subset S^{\mathrm{an}}
be an open ball containing
s
and
B\subset\mathscr{I}
be an open ball containing
z
such that
\Phi^{-1}(\pi_{\Gamma}(B))
(which is an open neighborhood of
s
in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
for the metric topology) is contained in
B_{0}
. Let
i:\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}\times D_{\mathbf{M}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
the restriction of the action map, which is a smooth map between smooth manifolds. For
p\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, we denote by
i_{p}:D_{\mathbf{M}}\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{P}}
mapping
x\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
to
i(p,x)
. We are tasked with finding an element
p\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
such that
i_{p}
is transverse to
\mathscr{I}
at some point
x\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
such that
i_{p}(x)\in B
.

By Lemma
8.5
, there exists a
p_{0}\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
such that
z\in p_{0}\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}
. Let
U
be a complex-analytic irreducible component of
\mathscr{I}\cap(p_{0}\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
containing
z
. By Proposition
7.7
, one has

\dim U=\dim(p_{0}\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})+\dim\mathscr{I}-\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}.

(8.2.1)

By Lemma
4.14
, this implies that
i_{p_{0}}
is transverse to
\mathscr{I}
at
z_{\mathbf{M}}:=p_{0}^{-1}\cdot z
. Theorem
4.15
then ensures the existence of an open neighborhood
\Omega
of
p_{0}
in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
and a smooth map
\sigma:\Omega\rightarrow D_{\mathbf{M}}
such that
\sigma(p_{0})=z_{\mathbf{M}}
and for any
p\in\Omega
, the map
i_{p}
is transverse to
\mathscr{I}
at
\sigma(p)
. Appealing to Lemma
4.14
again, one finds that for any
p\in\Omega
the translate
p\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathscr{I}
intersect transversally in
D_{\mathbf{P}}
along some complex-analytic irreducible component
U_{p}
containing
i_{p}(\sigma(p))
.

Combining the facts that
\sigma
and
i
are smooth, that
i(p_{0},\sigma(p_{0}))=z
and that
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
is analytically dense in
\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, we conclude to the existence of a
p\in\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cap\Omega
such that
i(p,\sigma(p))\in B
. By construction, we then have a
(p\mathbf{M}p^{-1},p\cdot D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
meeting
B_{0}
. As Hodge generic points are analytically dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
, this proves that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is analytically dense in
S^{\mathrm{an}}
, as desired.
∎

In particular this yields:

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

Assume that
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{-2}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})=0
which is equivalent to
W_{-2}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})=W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
. By Proposition
7.9
(i)
one knows that any
\mathbb{V}
-likely strict mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
satisfies
W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})=W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
, which under the above assumption ensures that
\exp(W_{-2}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))\subset\mathbf{M}
. Therefore, any
\mathbb{V}
-likely strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
is sufficiently unipotent and Theorem
8.6
finishes the proof.
∎

The full transverse mixed Hodge locus

As demonstrated in Propositions
6.2
and
6.3
, the
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness of a mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is not sufficient in general to guarantee the density of
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes},\mathbf{M})_{\mathrm{trans}}
or
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
in
S
for the metric topology. However, we can still show:

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible and graded-polarized integral variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be the generic mixed Hodge class of
\mathbb{V}
. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(i)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty;

(ii)

\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S
for the Zariski topology;

(iii)

There exists a strict
\mathbb{V}
-likely mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

The following notation will be convenient:

Notation 9.2
.

Let
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a mixed Hodge class and
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass, and let
W_{\bullet}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})
be the adjoint weight filtration of
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})
. We denote by
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})
the largest non-positive integer
k
such that
\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})\subsetneq\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
. This is well defined because
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a
strict
mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely and satisfies
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})\geqslant-1
. Then, there exists a strict mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely and sufficiently unipotent.

Proof.

Assume that
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})=0
, which means that the inclusion
\mathbf{M}/\mathbf{M}^{u}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u}
is strict. Choose a Levi subgroup
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{M}}
of
\mathbf{M}
. Then
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{M}}
is a closed reductive subgroup of
\mathbf{P}
, which normalizes
\mathbf{P}^{u}
. Let
h\in D_{\mathbf{M}}
. Since the connected
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\mathbf{L}:=\mathbf{P}^{u}\rtimes\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{M}}
of
\mathbf{P}
contains
\mathbf{M}
, there is, by Corollary
2.40
applied to
X=\{h\}
, an intermediate strict mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subset(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
with underlying inclusion the inclusion of
\mathbf{L}
in
\mathbf{P}
. Since
\mathbf{L}^{u}=\mathbf{P}^{u}
, one finds that
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})
is sufficiently unipotent. Besides
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})
is also
\mathbb{V}
-likely as it contains
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.

Assume that
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})=-1
. In particular
\mathbf{M}/\mathbf{M}^{u}=\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{u}
hence there is a connected
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{M}
which is a Levi subgroup of both
\mathbf{M}
and
\mathbf{P}
. Denote by
\mathfrak{u}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}^{u})
and
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{M}}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M}^{u})
. Let
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{M}}^{-1}
be a vector space supplement of
W_{-2}(\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{M}})
in
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{M}}
, and let
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{L}}=W_{-2}(\mathfrak{u})\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{M}}^{-1}
. It immediately follows from weight considerations that
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{L}}
is a Lie subalgebra of
\mathfrak{u}
, and from the fact that
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{M}}
and
W_{-2}\mathfrak{u}
are stable under
\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{G})
(seen as a subgroup of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathfrak{p})
) that
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{L}}
is stable under
\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{G})
. Hence
\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{L}}:=\exp(\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbf{L}})
is a subgroup of
\mathbf{P}^{u}
normalized by
\mathbf{G}
and containing
\mathbf{M}^{u}
. Hence, the connected
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\mathbf{L}:=\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{L}}\rtimes\mathbf{G}
of
\mathbf{P}
is well defined, and contains
\mathbf{M}
so that there is, by Corollary
2.40
applied to
X=\{h\}
, an intermediate strict mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subset(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
with underlying inclusion the inclusion of
\mathbf{L}
in
\mathbf{P}
. By construction
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})
is sufficiently unipotent. Besides
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})
is also
\mathbb{V}
-likely as it contains
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely.
∎

We can now turn to the:

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

The implication
(ii)\Rightarrow(i)
is obvious and the implication
(i)\Rightarrow(iii)
has been settled in Corollary
8.3
. It remains to prove that
(iii)\Rightarrow(ii)
. Let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely. We use the notations in Setting
7.8
.

We first claim that
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})\geqslant-2
. Indeed, by Proposition
7.9
(i), we have
W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})=W_{-3}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})
. In particular, for any
k<-2
the inclusion
\mathrm{Gr}_{W}^{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M})\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{W}^{k}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
is an equality. By definition, this means that
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})\geqslant-2
.

If
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})\geqslant-1
there exists by Lemma
9.3
a strict mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{L},D_{\mathbf{L}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
which is
\mathbb{V}
-likely and sufficiently unipotent, and Theorem
8.6
shows that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is dense in
S(\mathbb{C})
for the metric topology hence in particular for the Zariski topology. Therefore, we can assume that
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})=-2
.

By construction
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is a strict mixed Hodge subclass of
(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
, is
\mathbb{V}
-likely and satisfies
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})=-2
. By the assumptions that
k_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{M})=-2
it immediately follows that for
p+q\geqslant-1
the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
is an equality. Proposition
7.9
(iii)
then implies that for all
(p,q)\neq(-1,-1)
, the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
is an equality. Let
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{M}^{u}\rtimes\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
. It is clearly an element of
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
. The
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness condition for
\mathbf{N}
then shoes that

\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})\geqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}.

By construction
D_{\mathbf{M}}
is a fiber of
q_{\mathbf{N}}
hence
\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})=0
and since
\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})\leqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}
, one finds that
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}
is dominant, and has positive image because
\mathfrak{m}^{-1,-1}\subsetneq\mathfrak{p}^{-1,-1}
(otherwise we would have
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{P}
).

To conclude the proof, it suffices to show that the smooth locus of
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})
contains a point of the form
\phi_{i,\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}(\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{T},D_{\mathbf{T}}),(\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}})_{\mathbf{T}}})
for some strict torsion mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{T},D_{\mathbf{T}})
of
(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
. Indeed
\Phi_{\mathbf{N}}
being dominant, such a point would then be a transverse intersection (not contained in the singular locus) of
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})
with some Hodge subvariety of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}
. It is then a matter of unwrapping definitions to see that an irreducible component of the preimage of such a point by
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}
produces a transverse special subvariety of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
, hence showing that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}\neq\emptyset
and Theorem
8.1
would imply the sought for density in the Zariski topology.

By Proposition
2.42
, there exists a strict torsion mixed Hodge subclass
(\mathbf{T},D_{\mathbf{T}})
of
(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N})
. Let
\pi_{\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}:D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}
be the quotient by
\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}
and
h_{\mathbf{T}}\in D_{\mathbf{T}}
. We need to show that no complex-analytic subvariety
Y
of
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}
of strictly smaller dimension contains
\pi_{\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}((\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N})(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cdot h_{\mathbf{T}})
. This can be shown exactly as in the proof of Theorem
8.1
, which only uses the complex-analytic topology: if such a
Y
existed, its tangent space at a smooth point
y
would be forced to contain a real form of
T_{y}\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}
hence to fill the tangent space as it is a complex vector subspace, and this would contradict the assumption that
Y
has strictly smaller dimension.
∎

Emptiness of the transverse Hodge locus

Let
\mathbb{V}
be an admissible integral and graded-polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures on a smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Fix a base point
s\in S^{\mathrm{an}}
and let
(\mathbf{P},X_{\mathbf{P}},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be the generic mixed Hodge datum of
\mathbb{V}
at
s
, let
\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{H}_{s}
be the algebraic monodromy group of
\mathbb{V}
at
s
. Let
V=\mathbb{V}_{s}
and
W_{\bullet}=(\mathbb{W}_{\bullet})_{s}
. We see both
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{H}
as subgroups of the subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}}
of
\mathbf{GL}(V)
preserving the filtration
W_{\bullet}
. Denote by
\rho:\pi_{1}(S,s)\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}}(\mathbb{Q})
the monodromy representation associated to the local system
\mathbb{V}
at
s
. Denote by
\mathbf{P}_{0}
and
\mathbf{H}_{0}
the quotients of
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{H}
by their unipotent radicals, and denote by
\pi_{0}:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{0}
the quotient map.

By Theorem
3.11
, the group
\mathbf{H}
is a normal subgroup of
\mathbf{P}
hence
\mathfrak{h}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{H})
is a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge substructure of
\mathfrak{p}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
. By Pink’s axioms
\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{P})
respects the weight filtration and
\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{P}^{u})
makes the weight decrease of at least
1
hence for every integer
k\leqslant 0
we get a representation
R_{k}:\mathbf{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(W_{k}\mathfrak{h}/W_{k-1}\mathfrak{h})
from the adjoint representation of
\mathbf{P}
.

Definition 10.1
.

The
pure level of
\mathbb{V}
, denoted
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})
is the level of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
in the sense of
[
9
, Def. 4.15]
.

Theorem 10.2
.

Assume that
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})\geqslant 3
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
. If
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}\neq\emptyset
, there exists, for any transverse special subvariety
Z
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
, a strict normal subgroup
\mathbf{N}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{P})
of
\mathbf{P}
such that:

•

\mathbf{N}/\mathbf{N}^{u}=\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
;

•

for any
h\in D_{\mathbf{P}}
, the natural mixed Hodge structure induced on
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N})
is of type
\{(-1,-1),(0,0)\}
;

•

the quotient period map
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}:S^{\mathrm{an}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbb{N}}}
is dominant with positive dimensional image;

•

Z
is an irreducible component of the fiber of
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}
over a torsion point in
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbb{N}}}
.

In particular, one obtains a criterion for emptiness of
\mathbb{V}
:

Theorem 10.3
.

Assume that
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})\geqslant 3
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
. If furthermore one of the two following conditions hold

(A)

The trivial representation does not occur in
R_{-2}|_{\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}}
;

(B)

There is no dominant regular morphism
f:S\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
(hence
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}
) is empty.

Let us comment on the assumptions
(A)
and
(B)
. They are both intended to exclude the possibility of adding direct factors which are extensions in the category of integral variations of mixed Hodge structure of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{S}(0)
by
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{S}(1)^{n}
for some integer
n\geqslant 1
. For instance, assume that
\mathbb{V}
satisfies
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})\geqslant 3
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
but
(B)
fails. Assume that there exists a dominant regular morphism
f:S\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Let
\mathbb{E}
be the extension of
\mathbb{Z}_{S}(0)
by
\mathbb{Z}_{S}(1)
with period map
f
. Set
\mathbb{V}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{V}\oplus\mathbb{E}
and use all notations defined at the beginning of the section for
\mathbb{V}
with prime superscrips for
\mathbb{V}^{\prime}
. Then
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V}^{\prime})\geqslant 3
and
(\mathbf{P}^{\prime}_{0})^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
. However
\mathrm{HL}(S,(\mathbb{V}^{\prime})^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty due to the direct factor
\mathbb{E}
which has Zariski-dense transverse Hodge locus. Because of this factor, the trivial representation does occur as
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=(\mathbf{P}^{\prime}_{0})^{\mathrm{der}}
acts trivially on the unipotent part of the monodromy of
\mathbb{E}
, i.e.
(A)
fails. We do not know if such a situation can actually occur. In any case, the above result holds in the following interesting situations:

Corollary 10.4
.

Assume that
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})\geqslant 3
, that
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
and that
h^{-1,-1}_{\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})}=0
. Then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is empty.

Proof.

If
h^{-1,-1}_{\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})}=0
condition
(A)
of Theorem
10.3
is automatically satisfied because as shown in Lemma
10.8
, under
R_{-2}
the group
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
acts non-trivially on
(\mathrm{Gr}_{-2}^{W}\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}))^{p,-2-p}
for
p\in\mathbb{Z}-\{-1\}
. Hence the result.
∎

Corollary 10.5
.

Assume that
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})\geqslant 3
, that
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
and that
S
has a smooth compactification
\overline{S}
such that
\overline{S}-S
is either empty, of codimension at least
2
or a smooth irreducible divisor in
\overline{S}
. Then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is empty.

Proof.

Under one of these assumptions condition
(B)
of Theorem
10.3
is automatically satisfied. Hence the result.
∎

A grading element

In this subsection, we assume that
\mathbb{V}
is effective. Denote by
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{W}_{k}/\mathbb{W}_{k-1}
the graded of
\mathbb{V}
for
\mathbb{W}_{\bullet}
, which carries by definition a pure polarized integral variation of Hodge structures. Let
\mathrm{Gr}(V)=\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})_{s}
. By Lemma
2.15
, the groups
\mathbf{P}_{0}
and
\mathbf{H}_{0}
are naturally subgroups of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathrm{Gr}(V))=\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}}/(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}})^{u}
.

Lemma 10.6
.

The (connected)
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup
\mathbf{H}_{0}
of
\mathbf{GL}(\mathrm{Gr}(V))
is the algebraic monodromy group of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
at
s
.

Proof.

Since we assumed
\mathbb{V}
to be effective, the image of
\rho
is contained in
\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{Q})
hence
\mathbf{H}
is the smallest
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}}
whose
\mathbb{Q}
-points contain
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho)
. By definition, the monodromy representation of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
at
s
is the composite
\rho_{\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})}=\pi\circ\rho:\pi_{1}(S,s)\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathrm{Gr}(V))
where
\pi:\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathrm{Gr}(V))
is the quotient by
(\mathbf{P}_{W_{\bullet}})^{u}
. Since
\mathbf{H}
is a normal subgroup in
\mathbf{P}
by Theorem
3.11
its unipotent radical
\mathbf{H}^{u}
is
\mathbf{P}^{u}\cap\mathbf{H}
hence
\mathbf{H}_{0}=\pi(\mathbf{H})
. This readily implies that
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho_{\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})})\subset\mathbf{H}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
hence
\mathbf{H}_{0}
contains the algebraic monodromy group of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
. If the latter inclusion was not an equality, the image of
\rho_{\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})}
would be contained in the
\mathbb{Q}
-points of a strict
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic subgroup
\mathbf{H}^{\prime}_{0}
of
\mathbf{H}_{0}
, hence
\mathrm{im}\hskip 1.42271pt(\rho)
would be contained in the
\mathbb{Q}
-points of
\pi^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{\prime}_{0})
, contradicting the minimality of
\mathbf{H}
for this property since
\dim\pi^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{\prime}_{0})>\dim\mathbf{H}
.
∎

Let
x\in X_{\mathbf{P}}
be a bigraduation of the mixed Hodge structure on
\mathbb{V}_{s}
. Post-composition of
x
with the adjoint representation defines a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure on
\mathfrak{p}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P})
which by Pink’s axioms has only non-positive weights. Since
\mathbf{H}
is a normal subgroup of
\mathbf{P}
by Theorem
3.11
, its Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{H})
is a mixed sub-
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure of
\mathfrak{p}
, a bigraduation of which is given by
R_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x
where
R_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathbf{P}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathfrak{h})
is the sub-representation of the adjoint representation associated with
\mathbf{H}
. For
k\leqslant 0
the
k
-th graded-piece for the weight filtration
\mathfrak{h}_{k}=W_{k}\mathfrak{h}/W_{k-1}\mathfrak{h}
carries a weight
k
pure polarized
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structure whose Hodge decomposition we denote by

\mathfrak{h}_{k,\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{p,k-p}.

Combining the facts that
W_{-1}\mathfrak{h}=W_{-1}\mathfrak{p}
, that
W_{-1}\mathfrak{p}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}^{u})
and that
\mathbf{H}^{u}=\mathbf{H}\cap\mathbf{P}^{u}
, one finds that
\mathfrak{h}_{0}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{H}_{0})
. This is an ideal in the derived Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
of
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
.

By Pink’s axiom
(i)
, the morphism
(\pi_{0})_{\mathbb{C}}\circ x:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{0,\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
and we let
\bar{x}:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbf{P}_{0,\mathbb{R}}
be the corresponding morphism of real algebraic groups. Let
k\leqslant 0
be an integer. The image of
\mathbf{P}
by
R
preserves the weight filtration on
\mathfrak{h}
, hence
R
descends to a representation
R_{k}:\mathbf{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(\mathfrak{h}_{k})
, and the Hodge cocharacter associated with the pure Hodge structure on
\mathfrak{h}_{k}
is given by
(R_{k})_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\bar{x}
. Finally we let
r_{k}:\mathfrak{p}_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(\mathfrak{h}_{k})
be the derivative
dR_{k}:\mathfrak{p}_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(\mathfrak{h}_{k})
. Explicitly
r_{k}
can be described as the descent of the adjoint representation
W_{0}\mathfrak{h}\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(W_{k}\mathfrak{h})
using that the bracket is a morphism of Hodge structures so that
[W_{-1}\mathfrak{h},W_{k}\mathfrak{h}]\subset W_{k-1}\mathfrak{h}
. Note that by construction
r_{k}
is a morphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures of weight
0
and a morphism of Lie algebras, with
\mathrm{End}(\mathfrak{h}_{k})
endowed with the bracket induced by composition of endomorphisms.

Following a construction of Robles in
[
44
, Sect. 3]
, we now define a semi-simple element of
\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
whose eigenspaces under the representations
r_{k}
will be exactly the pieces of the Hodge decomposition of
\mathfrak{h}_{k}
. Let
S^{1}
be the subgroup of
\mathbb{S}
defined on real points as the unit circle in
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, parametrized as
\{e^{\frac{it}{2}}\hskip 2.84544pt:\hskip 2.84544ptt\in\mathbb{R}\}
. We identify
\mathrm{Lie}(S^{1})
with
\mathbb{R}
using this parametrization. Let
\mathtt{T}_{\mathbb{V}}
be the image of
1\in\mathrm{Lie}(S^{1})
under the derivative
d_{0}\bar{x}|_{\mathrm{Lie}(S^{1})}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathfrak{p}_{0,\mathbb{R}}
.

Lemma 10.7
.

The element
\mathtt{T}_{\mathbb{V}}
belongs to the derived subalgebra
(\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}})_{\mathbb{R}}
of
\mathfrak{p}_{0,\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}})
, it suffices to show that

\bar{x}(S^{1})\subset\mathbf{P}_{0,\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{der}}=\bigcap_{\chi:\mathbf{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}}\ker{\chi_{\mathbb{R}}}

where the intersection is over characters of
\mathbf{P}_{0}
. Let
\chi:\mathbf{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbf{GL}(L)
with
L
a dimension
1
rational vector space. Since
x
satisfies Pink’s axioms, the morphism
\chi_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\bar{x}
defines a pure Hodge structure on
L
. As a pure Hodge structure of rank
1
it must be of Hodge type
(l,l)
for some
l\in\mathbb{Z}
. Hence, for
e^{\frac{it}{2}}
in
S^{1}
the automorphism
\chi_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\bar{x}(e^{\frac{it}{2}})
of
L_{\mathbb{R}}
must be
e^{\frac{itl}{2}}e^{-\frac{itl}{2}}\mathrm{id}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}=\mathrm{id}_{L_{\mathbb{R}}}
. Therefore
\bar{x}(S^{1})\subset\ker{\chi_{\mathbb{R}}}
as desired.
∎

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
k,p\in\mathbb{Z}
be integers with
k\leqslant 0
. The endomorphism
(r_{k})_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathtt{T}_{\mathbb{V}})
leaves stable
\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{p,k-p}
and

(r_{k})_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathtt{T}_{\mathbb{V}})|_{\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{p,k-p}}=\frac{k-2p}{2}\cdot\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{p,k-p}}.

Proof.

Let
k,p\in\mathbb{Z}
be integers with
k\leqslant 0
. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the complexification of the automorphism
(R_{k})_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\bar{x}(e^{\frac{it}{2}})
of
\mathfrak{h}_{k,\mathbb{C}}
preserves
\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{p,k-p}
and acts on it as
e^{\frac{(k-p)it}{2}}e^{-\frac{pit}{2}}\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{k,p-k}}
. Taking the differential at
t=0
and unwrapping definitions gives the result.
∎

Emptiness in pure level at least
3

Proof of Theorems
10.2
and
10.3
.

As explained in the beginning of the Proof of Theorem
8.1
, one can assume that
\mathbb{V}
is effective without changing wether or not
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is empty. Therefore we assume in this proof that
\mathbb{V}
is effective. Assume for the sake of contradiction that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty and let
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})\subsetneq(\mathbf{P},D_{\mathbf{P}})
be a strict mixed Hodge subclass such that there exists an
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
-transverse special subvariety
Z
of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
. In particular
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
is
\mathbb{V}
-likely. We will use freely the notations of Setting
7.8
and established in Section
10.1
.

Step 1: First reductions.
By Proposition
7.9
(ii)
, the inclusion
\mathbf{M}/\mathbf{M}\cap\mathbf{H}\subset\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{H}
is an equality. Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H}}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{M}\cap\mathbf{H})
. It is a mixed
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge sub-structure of
\mathbf{H}
. In particular, for
k\leqslant 0
the graded-piece
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},k}:=W_{k}\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H}}/W_{k-1}\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H}}
is a pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge sub-structure of
\mathfrak{h}_{k}
. By Proposition
7.9
(i)
the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},k}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{k}
is an equality for every
k\leqslant-3
.

Step 2:
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},0}=\mathfrak{h}_{0}
.
The argument is similar to
[
9
, Proof of Theorem 7.1]
and we refer to
op. cit.
for more details. By Lemma
10.6
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
is the Lie algebra of the algebraic monodromy group
\mathbf{H}_{0}
of
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathbb{V})
which is a polarized variation of pure Hodge structures on the smooth and irreducible quasi-projective complex algebraic variety
S
. Therefore, by
[
9
, Prop. 7.4]
each non-compact factor of
\mathfrak{h}_{0,\mathbb{R}}
is generated in level
1
. By assumption, the
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
has level at least
3
. Arguing as follows for each
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factor of
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
, one can assume that
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-simple. Let
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathbb{R}}
be a non-compact
\mathbb{R}
-simple factor of
\mathfrak{h}_{0,\mathbb{R}}
which has level at least
3
. By Proposition
7.9
(iii)
one has that
h_{\mathfrak{m}}^{p,-p}=h_{\mathfrak{p}}^{p,-p}
for each
p\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|p|\geqslant-2
. Therefore, for
p\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|p|\geqslant-2
, one has that the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},0}^{p,-p}\cap\mathfrak{l}_{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathfrak{l}^{p,-p}
is an equality. Then
[
9
, Prop. 7.5]
implies that
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathbb{R}}\subset(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},0})_{\mathbb{R}}
, which in turn forces
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},0}=\mathfrak{h}_{0}
.

Step 3:
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},-1}=\mathfrak{h}_{-1}
.
Let
\mathfrak{u}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{-1}
be a (possibly zero)
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
-invariant (under
r_{-1}
) complement of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},-1}
in
\mathfrak{h}_{-1}
and
r_{\mathfrak{u}}:\mathfrak{h}_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(\mathfrak{u})
the corresponding sub-representation. By Proposition
7.9
(iii)
, one has
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{-1}^{-1,0}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{-1}^{0,-1}
. In particular, for every
p\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|p|\geqslant-2
one has that
(r_{\mathfrak{u}})_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{p,-p})=\{0\}
. In other words,

\bigoplus_{p\in\mathbb{Z},|p|\geqslant-2}\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{p,-p}\subset\ker(r_{\mathfrak{u}}).

(10.2.1)

Since
r_{\mathfrak{u}}
is a morphism of both Lie algebras and pure
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge structures, one has that
\ker(r_{\mathfrak{u}})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge-Lie subalgebra of
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
. Arguing as in the end of the last paragraph using
[
9
, Prop. 7.5]
and the containment (
10.2.1
), one finds that
\ker(r_{\mathfrak{u}})=\mathfrak{h}_{0}
. In particular one finds that
(r_{-1})_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathtt{T}_{\mathbb{V}})|_{\mathfrak{u}}=0
. By Lemma
10.8
, this forces
\mathfrak{u}=0
as desired: the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},-1}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{-1}
is an equality.

Step 4: End of proof.
Summing up, we have shown that for every
k\neq-2
, the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},k}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{k}
is an equality. Using furthermore Proposition
7.9
(iii)
, we find that for all
(p,q)\neq(-1,-1)
the inclusion
\mathfrak{m}^{p,q}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{p,q}
is an equality. We now show that we are indeed in the situation described in Theorem
10.2
and explain how this contradicts assumptions
(A)
and
(B)
.

Repeating the proof of
Step 3
one finds that
\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathfrak{h}_{0}
acts trivially on a subrepresentation of
r_{-2}|_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}}
defined as an
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
-invariant complement of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{H},-2}
in
\mathfrak{h}_{-2}
. Since this complement must be non-zero (because otherwise one would have
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{p}
, hence
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{P}
which was excluded) this shows that the trivial representation occurs in
r_{-2}|_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}}
hence in
R_{-2}|_{\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}}
. This contradicts assumption
(A)
.

On the other hand, since
\mathbf{P}_{0}=\mathbf{M}/\mathbf{M}^{u}
there exists a common Levi subgroup
\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{M}
to
\mathbf{M}
and
\mathbf{P}
. Let
\mathbf{N}=\mathbf{M}^{u}\rtimes\mathbf{G}
which is by construction a normal subgroup of
\mathbf{P}
whose radical is unipotent. The
\mathbb{V}
-likeliness condition of for
(\mathbf{M},D_{\mathbf{M}})
and
\mathbf{N}
then shoes that

\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})+\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})\geqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}.

By construction
D_{\mathbf{M}}
is a fiber of
q_{\mathbf{N}}
hence
\dim q_{\mathbf{N}}(D_{\mathbf{M}})=0
and since
\dim\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}(S^{\mathrm{an}})\leqslant\dim D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}
, one finds that
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}
is dominant (with positive dimensional image by construction of
\mathbf{N}
). By Theorem
3.29
it follows that the Hodge variety is the analytification of a quasi-projective algebraic variety and that
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}
is the analytification of a regular morphism between quasi-projective varieties. Since the Hodge structure on
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N})
is of type
\{(0,0),(-1,-1)\}
by construction, the Hodge variety
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}
is isomorphic as an algebraic variety to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
for some
n\geqslant 1
. Therefore, there exists a dominant regular morphism
f:S\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
such that
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}=f^{\mathrm{an}}
. Postcomposition of
f
with any projection
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
gives a dominant regular morphism
S\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
, contradicting assumption
(B)
.

Finally, we have just shown that the group
\mathbf{N}
, constructed under the assumption that
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{trans}}
is non-empty, indeed satisfies the properties in Theorem
10.2
hence the claimed alternative. Besides, by construction
Z
is the preimage under
\Phi_{/\mathbf{N}}
of some torsion point in
\mathscr{D}_{(\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{N},D_{\mathbf{P}}/\mathbf{N}),\Gamma_{/\mathbf{N}}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
.
∎

Algebraicity of the Hodge locus

From the emptiness criterion proved above, we can conclude, using Corollary
5.14
of the Geometric Zilber-Pink theorem, to a finiteness statement for the part of the Hodge locus that "does not come from special points". Let
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{f-pos}}
be the union of special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
which have
\mathbb{V}
-factorwise positive dimension. The following is a partial generalization of
[
36
, Thm. 3.2]
.

Theorem 10.9
.

Assume that
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{H}_{0}
and
\mathrm{pl}(\mathbb{V})\geqslant 3
and at least one of the following holds

(A)

The trivial representation does not occur in
R_{-2}|_{\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}}
;

(B)

There is no dominant regular morphism
f:S\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Then
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{f-pos}}
is a Zariski-closed subset of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
.

Proof.

Let
\{Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{l}\}
be the set of special subvarieties of
S
for
\mathbb{V}
as in the statement of Corollary
5.14
. Let
\mathscr{S}
be the set subvarieties of
S
which are special and have
\mathbb{V}
-factorwise positive dimension and are maximal for these properties with respect to the inclusion. We wish to prove that
\mathscr{S}
is a finite set.

Let
Z\in\mathscr{S}
. By Theorem
10.3
, we have
\mathrm{HL}(S,\mathbb{V}^{\otimes})_{\mathrm{typ}}=\emptyset
so that
Z
is an atypical special subvariety of
S
with
\mathbb{V}
-factorwise positive dimension. By Corollary
5.14
, there exists a
k\in\{1,\cdots,l\}
such that
Z\subset Z_{k}
. This forces
Z_{k}
to have
\mathbb{V}
-factorwise positive dimension. By maximality of
Z
, it follows that
Z=Z_{k}
. We have shown that
\mathscr{S}\subset\{Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{l}\}
which implies the desired finiteness.
∎
